Appendix D
Proof

In this section, we provide the proof of lemmas and theorems in the main paper.

Proof of Theorem
4.2
:

Given a symbolic database
\Gamma
and an integrity constraint
\mathcal{C}
, consider a formula
\Phi
such that
\Gamma\vdash\mathcal{C}\rightsquigarrow\Phi
. If
\Phi
is satisfiable, then the model of
\Phi
corresponds to a database consistent with
\Gamma
that satisfies
\mathcal{C}
. If
\Phi
is unsatisfiable, then no database consistent with
\Gamma
satisfies
\mathcal{C}
.

∎

Proof.

By structural induction on
\mathcal{C}
.

(1)

Base case:
\mathcal{C}=\text{PK}(R,\vec{a})
.

Suppose
\Gamma(R)=[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]
and
m=|\vec{a}|
by Figure
9
and, for every tuple
t_{i}\in

\Gamma(R)
, it has
t_{i}.a_{k}\neq\text{Null}
where
a_{k}
denotes the attribute and
1\leq k\leq m
.
Also,
\Phi_{2}
in Figure
9
ensures no two tuples
t_{i}
and
t_{j}
where
t_{i}\in\Gamma(R)
and
t_{j}\in\Gamma(R)
such that
\land_{k=1}^{m}t_{i}.a_{k}=t_{j}.a_{k}
is true.
Therefore, it holds that if
\Phi
is satisfiable, we have a model of
\Phi
corresponds to a database consistent with
\Gamma
that satisfies
\text{PK}(R,\vec{a})
as
\Phi_{1}\land\Phi_{2}
satisfies
\forall t_{i}\in\Gamma(R).t_{i}.a\neq\text{Null}\land\forall t_{i}\in\Gamma(R).\forall t_{j}\in\Gamma(R).i\neq j\to\neg(\land_{k=1}^{m}t_{i}.a_{k}=t_{j}.a_{k})
, thus ensuring the model conforms to primary key properties that 1) attributes
\vec{a}
of every tuple in
\Gamma(R)
are non-
NULL
, and 2) there are no two tuples in
\Gamma(R)
such that their values of
\vec{a}
are identical. And on the contrary, if
\Phi
is unsatisfiable, then it is necessarily true that there exists no database consistent with
\Gamma
that can satisfy
\text{PK}(R,\vec{a})
.

(2)

Base case:
\mathcal{C}=\text{FK}(R_{1},a_{1},R_{2},a_{2})
.

Suppose
\Gamma(R_{1})=[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]
and
\Gamma(R_{2})=[t_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,t_{m}^{\prime}]
, by Figure
9
,
\Phi
ensures for any tuple
t_{i}\in\Gamma(R_{1})
,
t_{i}.a_{1}\in[t_{1}^{\prime}.a_{2},\ldots,t_{m}^{\prime}.a_{2}]
.
Therefore, it holds that if
\Phi
is satisfiable, we have a model of
\Phi
corresponds to a database consistent with
\Gamma
that satisfies
\text{FK}(R_{1},a_{1},R_{2},a_{2})
as
\Phi
satisfies
\forall t_{i}\in\Gamma(R_{1}).t_{i}.a_{1}\in[t_{1}^{\prime}.a_{2},\ldots,t_{m}^{\prime}.a_{2}]
(the value of
a_{1}
of every tuple in
\Gamma(R_{1})
references the value
a_{2}
of any tuple in
\Gamma(R_{2})
); also, if
\Phi
is unsatisfiable, then it is necessarily true that there exists no database consistent with
\Gamma
satisfies
\text{FK}(R_{1},a_{1},R_{2},a_{2})
.

(3)

Base case:
\mathcal{C}=\text{NotNull}(R,a)
.

Suppose
\Gamma(R)=[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]
, by Figure
9
,
\Phi
ensures for any tuple
t_{i}
in
\Gamma(R)
,
t_{i}.a\neq\text{Null}
.
Therefore, it holds that if
\Phi
is satisfiable, we have a model of
\Phi
corresponding to a database consistent with
\Gamma
where
\forall t_{i}\in\Gamma(R).t_{i}.a\neq\text{Null}
, which satisfies that the value of
a
of every tuple in
\Gamma(R)
is non-
NULL
, and also if
\Phi
is unsatisfiable, then it is necessarily true that there exists no database consistent with
\Gamma
satisfies
\text{NotNull}(R,a)
.

(4)

Base case:
\mathcal{C}=\text{Check}(R,\psi)
.

Suppose
\Gamma(R)=[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]
, by Figure
9
,
\Phi
ensures for any tuple
t_{i}
, the predicate
\llbracket\psi\rrbracket_{t_{i}}
evaluates to
\top
.
Therefore, it holds that if
\Phi
is satisfiable, the model of
\Phi
corresponds to a database consistent with
\Gamma
that satisfies
\forall t_{i}\in\Gamma(R).\llbracket\psi\rrbracket_{t_{i}}=\top
that conforms to the
Check
constraint that every tuple in
\Gamma(R)
satisfies
\psi
, and if
\Phi
is unsatisfiable, then it is necessarily true that there exists no database consistent with
\Gamma
satisfies
\text{Check}(R,\psi)
.

(5)

Base case:
\mathcal{C}=\text{Inc}(R,a,v)
.

Suppose
\Gamma(R)=[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]
, by Figure
9
,
\Phi_{2}
foremost constrains the attribute
t_{i}.a
is non-
NULL
for every tuple
t_{i}\in\Gamma(R)
.
\Phi_{1}
then ensures for any tuple
t_{i}
, the attribute
t_{i}.a=v+i-1
, where
v
is the initial value.
Therefore, it holds that if
\Phi
is satisfiable, we have a model of
\Phi
corresponding to a database consistent with
\Gamma
that satisfies
\forall t_{i}\in\Gamma(R).t_{i}.a\neq\texttt{NULL}\land t_{i}.a=v+i-1
and hence ensures the value of
a
of every tuple in
\Gamma(R)
is incrementally increased by 1 from the initial value
v
; if
\Phi
is unsatisfiable, then it is necessarily true that there exists no database consistent with
\Gamma
satisfies
\text{Inc}(R,a,v)
.

(6)

Inductive case:
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}=\mathcal{C}\land\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
.

By the base cases shown above, the Theorem for the inductive case
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}=\mathcal{C}\land\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
can be proved.

∎

Proof of Theorem
4.5
:

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a database over schema
\mathcal{S}
and
o_{R}
be the output relation of running query
Q
over
\mathcal{D}
. Consider a symbolic database
\Gamma
over
\mathcal{S}
, a symbolic relation
R
and a formula
\Phi
such that
\Gamma\vdash Q\hookrightarrow R
and
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\leadsto\Phi
. For any interpretation
\mathcal{I}
such that
\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)=\mathcal{D}
, there exists an extension
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{I}
such that (1) the result of query
Q
over
\mathcal{D}
is
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(R)
, and (2) the database
\mathcal{D}
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
jointly satisfy
\Phi
, i.e.,

\begin{array}[]{l}(\Gamma\vdash Q\hookrightarrow R)\land(\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\leadsto\Phi)\land(\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)=\mathcal{D})\Rightarrow\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\exists\mathcal{I}^{\prime}\sqsupseteq\mathcal{I}.\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(R)=\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}\land(\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}[o_{R}\mapsto\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(R)]\models\Phi)\end{array}

Prove by structural induction on
Q
.
15
15

15

To be concise, we widely use a function
ptr
in this proof section. The function
ptr
takes as input an index
i
and returns an index
j
s.t.
\mathcal{I}(t_{j})=x_{i}
where
x_{i}\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
and
t_{j}\in R
.

(1)

Base case:
Q=T
.

R=\Gamma(T)
by Figure
11
.

\Phi=\top
by Figure
12
.

Take
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}=\mathcal{I}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}=\mathcal{D}[o_{R}\mapsto\mathcal{I}(R)]
.
Obviously, we have (a)
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(R)=\mathcal{I}(R)=\mathcal{I}(\Gamma(T))=\mathcal{D}(T)=\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
because
\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}=\mathcal{D}(T)
by Figure
18
, (b)
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\top
.

Thus, Theorem
4.5
for the inductive case
Q=T
is proved.

(2)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime}=\Pi_{L}(Q)
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q:[a_{1},\ldots,a_{m}]
by Figure
10
;

\mathcal{S}\vdash\sigma_{L}(Q):[a_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,a_{l}^{\prime}]
where
l=|L|
by Figure
10
;

\Gamma\vdash Q\hookrightarrow R
where
R
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]
by Figure
11
;

\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\leadsto\Phi
by Figure
18
;

\llbracket\Pi_{L}(Q)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}=\textsf{ite}(\text{hasAggr}(L),[\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}}],\textsf{map}(\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}},\lambda x.\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},x}))
by Figure
17
.

By the inductive hypothesis, we have
\mathcal{I}(R)=\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
and
\mathcal{I},\mathcal{D}\models\Phi
.

Let us discuss
\text{hasAggr}(L)
in two cases.

(a)

If
\text{hasAggr}(L)
is true, then
\Gamma\vdash\Pi_{L}(Q)\hookrightarrow R^{\prime}
where
R^{\prime}
only has one symbolic tuple
[t_{1}^{\prime}]
by Figure
11
,
\llbracket\Pi_{L}(Q)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}=[\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}}]
by Figure
17
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash\Pi_{L}(Q)\leadsto\Phi\land\land_{i=1}^{l}\Phi_{i}^{\prime}\land\neg\text{Del}(t_{1}^{\prime})
where
\Phi_{i}^{\prime}=\llbracket a_{j}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{1}^{\prime}]}=\llbracket a_{j}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\vec{t}}
by Figure
12
.

Take
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(t_{i})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{1}^{\prime})=\bot
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{1}^{\prime})=[(\text{ToString}(a_{1}^{\prime}),\llbracket a_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},\mathcal{I}(R)}),\ldots,(\text{ToString}(a_{l}^{\prime}),\llbracket a_{l}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},\mathcal{I}(R)})]
.

By the definition of
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
and the inductive hypothesis
\mathcal{I}(R)=\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
, we know

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\Pi_{L}(Q))&=&[(\text{ToString}(a_{1}^{\prime}),\llbracket a_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},\mathcal{I}(R)}),\ldots,(\text{ToString}(a_{l}^{\prime}),\llbracket a_{l}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},\mathcal{I}(R)})]\\
&=&[(\text{ToString}(a_{1}^{\prime}),\llbracket a_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}}),\ldots,(\text{ToString}(a_{l}^{\prime}),\llbracket a_{l}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}})]\\
&=&[\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}}]\\
&=&\llbracket\Pi_{L}(Q)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}\end{array}

Let
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}=\mathcal{D}[o_{\Pi_{L}(Q)}\mapsto\llbracket\Pi_{L}(Q)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}]
.
By Lemma
D.5
, we know
\mathcal{I}(\llbracket a\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})=\llbracket a\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}
and , therefore,
\mathcal{I},\mathcal{D}\models\llbracket a^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}=\llbracket a\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}\Leftrightarrow\mathcal{I},\mathcal{D}\models\llbracket a^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\llbracket a\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}
.

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}&\models&\land_{j=1}^{l}\llbracket a_{j}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),[\mathcal{I}(t_{1}^{\prime})]}=\llbracket a_{j}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}([t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}])}\\
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}&\models&\land_{j=1}^{l}\llbracket a_{j}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{1}^{\prime}]}=\llbracket a_{j}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]}\\
\end{array}

Thus, we have
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\Phi\land\land_{i=1}^{l}\Phi_{i}^{\prime}\land\neg\text{Del}(t_{1}^{\prime})

(b)

If
\text{hasAggr}(L)
is false, then
\Gamma\vdash\Pi_{L}(Q)\hookrightarrow R^{\prime}
where
R^{\prime}
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,t_{n}^{\prime}]
by Figure
11
,
\llbracket\Pi_{L}(Q)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}=\textsf{map}(\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}},\lambda x.\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},x})
by Figure
17
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash\Pi_{L}(Q)\leadsto\Phi\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}^{\prime}
where
\Phi_{i}^{\prime}=\land_{j=1}^{l}\llbracket a_{j}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{i}^{\prime}]}=\llbracket a_{j}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{i}]}\land\text{Del}(t_{i}^{\prime})\leftrightarrow\text{Del}(t_{i})
by Figure
12
.

Take
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(t_{i})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime})\leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i}^{\prime})=[(\text{ToString}(a_{1}^{\prime}),\llbracket a_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]}),\ldots,(\text{ToString}(a_{l}^{\prime}),\llbracket a_{l}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]})]=\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]}
.

Let
x_{i}\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
where
1\leq i\leq c
and
c=|\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}|
, then we know
x_{i}\in\mathcal{I}(R)
because
\mathcal{I}(R)=\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
,
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(x_{i})=\bot
.
Further, for any tuple
x_{i}\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
, we have two symbolic tuples
t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}\in Q
and
t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime}\in\Pi_{L}(Q)
s.t.
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(x_{i})=\bot
and
\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[x_{i}]}=\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[\mathcal{I}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)})]}=\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime})
.

By the semantics of
map
, we have

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\llbracket\Pi_{L}(Q)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}&=&\textsf{map}(\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}},\lambda x.\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},x})\\
&=&[\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[x_{1}]},\ldots,\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[x_{c}]}]\\
&=&[\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[\mathcal{I}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(1)})]},\ldots,\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[\mathcal{I}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(c)})]}]\\
&=&[\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(1)}^{\prime}),\ldots,\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(c)}^{\prime})]\\
&=&\mathcal{I}^{\prime}([t_{\textsf{ptr}(1)}^{\prime},\ldots,t_{\textsf{ptr}(c)}^{\prime}])\\
&=&\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\Pi_{L}(Q))\\
\end{array}

Let
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}=\mathcal{D}[o\mapsto\llbracket\Pi_{\phi}(Q)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}]
.
Since
\llbracket a_{j}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[\mathcal{I}(t_{i}^{\prime})]}=\llbracket a_{j}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]}\Leftrightarrow\llbracket a_{j}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{i}^{\prime}]}=\llbracket a_{j}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{i}]}
and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})
, we know
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\Phi_{i}^{\prime}
and, therefore,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\Phi\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}^{\prime}
.

Hence,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\Phi\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}^{\prime}
by
2(a)
and
2(b)
and Theorem
4.5
for the inductive case
Q^{\prime}=\Pi_{L}(Q)
is proved.

(3)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime}=\sigma_{\phi}(Q)
.

Suppose that
\Gamma\vdash Q\hookrightarrow R
where
R
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]
,
\Gamma\vdash\sigma_{\phi}(Q)\hookrightarrow R^{\prime}
where
R^{\prime}
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,t_{n}^{\prime}]
by Figure
11
;
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\leadsto\Phi
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash\sigma_{\phi}(Q)\leadsto\Phi\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}^{\prime}
where
\Phi_{i}^{\prime}=(\neg\text{Del}(t_{i})\land\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{i}]}=\top)\to t_{i}^{\prime}=t\land(\text{Del}(t_{i})\lor\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{i}]}\neq\top)\to\text{Del}(t_{i}^{\prime})
by Figure
18
.

By the inductive hypothesis, we have
\mathcal{I}(R)=\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
and
\mathcal{I},\mathcal{D}\models\Phi
.

Take
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(t_{i})
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime})\leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\lor\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]}\neq\top
.

For any tuple
x_{i}\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
, we have a symbolic tuple
t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}\in R
s.t.
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(x_{i})\\

=\bot
and
\mathcal{I}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)})=\mathcal{I}(x_{i})
.
If
\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[x_{i}]}=\top
, then
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)})\lor\\

\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[\mathcal{I}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)})]}\neq\top=\bot
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)})=x_{i}
.
Otherwise,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)})\lor(\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[\mathcal{I}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)})]}\neq\top)=\top
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)})=x_{i}
.
Therefore, for any
x_{i}^{\prime\prime}\in\llbracket\sigma_{\phi}(Q)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
, we have a symbolic tuple
t_{ptr(i)}^{\prime}\in R^{\prime}
.

\llbracket\sigma_{\phi}(Q)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}=\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime})
is proved.

Let
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}=\mathcal{D}[o_{R^{\prime}}\mapsto\llbracket\sigma_{\phi}(Q)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}]
.
By Lemma
D.1
, we know
\mathcal{I}(\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[x]})=\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}([x])}=\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[\mathcal{I}(x)]}
and, therefore,
\mathcal{I},\mathcal{D}\models\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[x]}\Leftrightarrow\mathcal{I},\mathcal{D}\models\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[\mathcal{I}(x)]}
and
\mathcal{I},\mathcal{D}\models\Phi_{i}^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow\mathcal{I},\mathcal{D}\models(\neg\text{Del}(t_{i})\land\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]}=\top)\to t_{i}^{\prime}=t_{i}\land(\text{Del}(t_{i})\lor\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]}\neq\top)\to\text{Del}(t_{i}^{\prime})
.

For any
t_{i}\in R
, let us discuss it in two cases.

(a)

If
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\top
, then, by the definition of
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
, we have
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\top
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\lor(\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]}\neq\top)=\top
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(t_{i})
,

\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models(\neg\text{Del}(t_{i})\land\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i})]}=\top)\to t_{i}^{\prime}=t_{i}

and

\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models(\text{Del}(t_{i})\lor\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i})]}\neq\top)\to\text{Del}(t_{i}^{\prime})

Thus,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\Phi_{i}^{\prime}
.

(b)

If
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\bot
, then, by the definition of
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
, we have
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\bot
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\lor(\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]}\neq\top)=\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]}\neq\top
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(t_{i})
.

Let us further discuss
\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]}
in two cases.

(b1)

If
\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]}=\top
, then
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime})=\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]}\neq\top=\bot
, we have

\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models(\neg\text{Del}(t_{i})\land\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]}=\top)\to t_{i}^{\prime}=t_{i}

and

\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models(\text{Del}(t_{i})\lor\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]}\neq\top)\to\text{Del}(t_{i}^{\prime})

(b2)

If
\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]}\neq\top
, then
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime})=\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]}\neq\top=\top
, we have

\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models(\neg\text{Del}(t_{i})\land\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]}=\top)\to t_{i}^{\prime}=t_{i}

and

\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models(\text{Del}(t_{i})\lor\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]}\neq\top)\to\text{Del}(t_{i}^{\prime})

Hence,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\Phi_{i}^{\prime}
by
3((b))(b1)
and
3((b))(b2)
.

Thus,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\Phi\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}^{\prime}
by
3(a)
and
3(b)
and Theorem
4.5
for the inductive case
Q^{\prime}=\sigma_{\phi}(Q)
is proved.

(4)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime}=\rho_{T}(Q)
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q:[a_{1},\ldots,a_{p}]
by Figure
10
;
\Gamma\vdash Q\hookrightarrow R
where
R
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]
,
\Gamma\vdash\rho_{T}(Q)\hookrightarrow R^{\prime}
where
R^{\prime}
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,t_{n}^{\prime}]
by Figure
11
;
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\leadsto\Phi
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash\Phi\land\land_{i=1}^{n}t_{i}^{\prime}=t_{i}
by Figure
18
.

By the inductive hypothesis, we have
\mathcal{I}(R)=\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
and
\mathcal{I},\mathcal{D}\models\Phi
.

Take
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime})\leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(t_{i})
, and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i}^{\prime})=\\

[(\text{rename}(R,\text{ToString}(a_{1})),\llbracket a_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]}),\ldots,(\text{rename}(R,\text{ToString}(a_{p})),\llbracket a_{p}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]})]
.

Let
x_{i}\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
where
1\leq i\leq n
, then we have
t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}\in\mathcal{I}(R)
because
\mathcal{I}(R)=\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
.
Also, by the semantics of
map
, we know

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\llbracket\rho_{T}(Q)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}&=&\textsf{map}(\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}},\lambda x.\textsf{map}(x,\lambda(n,v).(v,\text{rename}(T,n))))\\
&=&\textsf{map}([x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}],\lambda x.\textsf{map}(x,\lambda(n,v).(v,\text{rename}(T,n))))\\
&=&[\\
&&\quad[(\text{rename}(T,\text{ToString}(a_{1})),\llbracket a_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[x_{1}]}),\ldots,\\
&&\qquad\qquad(\text{rename}(T,\text{ToString}(a_{p})),\llbracket a_{p}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[x_{1}]})]\\
&&\quad\ldots,\\
&&\quad[(\text{rename}(T,\text{ToString}(a_{1})),\llbracket a_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[x_{n}]}),\ldots,\\
&&\qquad\qquad(\text{rename}(T,\text{ToString}(a_{p})),\llbracket a_{p}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[x_{n}]})]\\
&&]\\
\end{array}

By the definition of
ptr
, we know, for any
x_{i}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\rho_{T}(Q)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
, there exists a symbolic tuple
t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime}\in R^{\prime}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime})=x_{i}^{\prime}
.
Further,
\llbracket\rho_{T}(Q)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}=[x_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,x_{n}^{\prime}]=[\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(1)}^{\prime}),\ldots,

\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(n)}^{\prime})]=\mathcal{I}^{\prime}([t_{\textsf{ptr}(1)}^{\prime},\ldots,t_{\textsf{ptr}(n)}^{\prime}])=\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(R^{\prime})
.

Let
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}=\mathcal{D}[T\mapsto\llbracket\rho_{T}(Q)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}]
.
For any symbolic tuple
t_{i}\in R
and
t_{i}\in R
, we know
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})
and
t_{i}
is equal to
t_{i}^{\prime}
in terms of every attribute.
Therefore,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models t_{i}^{\prime}=t_{i}
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\Phi\land\land_{i=1}^{n}t_{i}^{\prime}=t_{i}
.

Thus, Theorem
4.5
for the inductive case
Q^{\prime}=\rho_{T}(Q)
is proved.

(5)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\times Q^{\prime}
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q:[a_{1},\ldots,a_{p}]
,
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q^{\prime}:[a_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,a_{q}^{\prime}]
by Figure
10
;
\Gamma\vdash Q\hookrightarrow R
where
R
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]
,
\Gamma\vdash Q^{\prime}\hookrightarrow R^{\prime}
where
R^{\prime}
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,t_{m}^{\prime}]
,
\Gamma\vdash Q\times Q^{\prime}\hookrightarrow R^{\prime\prime}
where
R^{\prime\prime}
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1,1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{n,m}^{\prime\prime}]
by Figure
11
;
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\leadsto\Phi
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q^{\prime}\leadsto\Phi^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\times Q^{\prime}\leadsto\Phi\land\Phi^{\prime}\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\land_{j=1}^{m}\Phi^{\prime\prime}_{i,j}
where
\Phi^{\prime\prime}_{i,j}=(\neg\text{Del}(t_{i})\land\neg\text{Del}(t_{j}^{\prime}))\to(\neg\text{Del}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})\land\land_{k=1}^{p}\llbracket a_{k}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime}]}=\llbracket a_{k}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{i}]}\land\land_{k=1}^{q}\llbracket a_{k}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime}]}=\llbracket a_{k}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{j}^{\prime}]})\land(\text{Del}(t_{i})\lor\text{Del}(t_{j}^{\prime})\to\text{Del}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime}))
by Figure
18
.

By the inductive hypothesis, we have
\mathcal{I}(R)=\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
,
\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime})=\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
and
\mathcal{I},\mathcal{D}\models\Phi\land\Phi^{\prime}
.

Take
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(t_{i})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})\leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{j}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(t_{j}^{\prime})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\lor\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})=\textsf{merge}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i}),\mathcal{I}(t_{j}^{\prime}))
.

Let
x_{i}\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
,
x_{j}^{\prime}\in\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
where
1\leq i\leq c\leq n
,
1\leq j\leq d\leq m
,
c=|\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}|
and
d=|\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}|
, then we have
x_{i}\in\mathcal{I}(R)
and
x_{j}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime})
because
\mathcal{I}(R)=\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
and
\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime})=\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
,
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(x_{i})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(x_{j}^{\prime})=\bot
.
Also, by the semantics of
foldl
, we know

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\llbracket Q\times Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}&=&\textsf{foldl}(\lambda xs.\lambda x.\textsf{append}(xs,\textsf{map}(\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}},\lambda y.\textsf{merge}(x,y))),[],\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}})\\
&=&\textsf{foldl}(\lambda xs.\lambda x.\textsf{append}(xs,\\
&&\qquad[\textsf{merge}(x,x_{1}^{\prime}),\ldots,\textsf{merge}(x,x_{q}^{\prime})]),[],\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}})\\
&=&[\\
&&\qquad[\textsf{merge}(x_{1},x_{1}^{\prime}),\ldots,\textsf{merge}(x_{1},x_{q}^{\prime})],\\
&&\qquad\ldots,\\
&&\qquad[\textsf{merge}(x_{p},x_{1}^{\prime}),\ldots,\textsf{merge}(x_{p},x_{q}^{\prime})]\\
&&]\\
&=&[[x_{1,1},\ldots,x_{1,d}],\ldots,[x_{c,1},\ldots,x_{c,d}]]\\
&=&\mathcal{I}^{\prime}([[x_{1,1},\ldots,x_{1,d}],\ldots,[x_{c,1},\ldots,x_{c,d}]])\\
\end{array}

By the definition of
ptr
, we know, for any
x_{i,j}^{\prime\prime}\in\llbracket Q\times Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
, there exists a symbolic tuple
t_{\textsf{ptr}(i),\textsf{ptr}(j)}^{\prime\prime}\in R^{\prime\prime}
as
x_{i,j}
is a concatenation of
x_{i}\in Q
and
x_{j}\in Q^{\prime}
.
Conversely, for any symbolic tuple
t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime}\in R^{\prime\prime}-\llbracket Q\times Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})=\bot
because
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\lor\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})=\bot
.
Therefore,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(R^{\prime\prime})=\llbracket Q\times Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
.

Let
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}=\mathcal{D}[o_{Q^{\prime\prime}}\mapsto\llbracket Q\times Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}]
.
For any
t_{i}\in R
and
t_{j}^{\prime}\in R^{\prime}
, let us discuss them in four cases.

(a)

If
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})=\top
, then, by the definition of
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
, we have
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\top
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})=\top
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\lor\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})\\

=\top
,
\llbracket a_{k}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})]}=\llbracket a_{k}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]}
and
\llbracket a_{k}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})]}=\llbracket a_{k}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}(t_{j}^{\prime})]}
,

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}&\models&(\neg\text{Del}(t_{i})\land\neg\text{Del}(t_{j}^{\prime}))\to(\neg\text{Del}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})\land\land_{k=1}^{p}\llbracket a_{k}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})]}=\llbracket a_{k}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i})]}\\
&&\qquad\land\land_{k=1}^{q}\llbracket a_{k}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})]}=\llbracket a_{k}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{j}^{\prime})]})\end{array}

and

\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\text{Del}(t_{i})\lor\text{Del}(t_{j}^{\prime})\to\text{Del}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})

Thus,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\Phi_{i,j}^{\prime\prime}
.

(b)

If
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\bot
and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})=\top
, then, by the definition of
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
, we have
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\bot
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})=\top
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\lor\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})\\

=\top
,
\llbracket a_{k}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})]}=\llbracket a_{k}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]}
and
\llbracket a_{k}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})]}=\llbracket a_{k}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}(t_{j}^{\prime})]}
,

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}&\models&(\neg\text{Del}(t_{i})\land\neg\text{Del}(t_{j}^{\prime}))\to(\neg\text{Del}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})\land\land_{k=1}^{p}\llbracket a_{k}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})]}=\llbracket a_{k}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i})]}\\
&&\qquad\land\land_{k=1}^{q}\llbracket a_{k}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})]}=\llbracket a_{k}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{j}^{\prime})]})\end{array}

and

\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\text{Del}(t_{i})\lor\text{Del}(t_{j}^{\prime})\to\text{Del}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})

Thus,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\Phi_{i,j}^{\prime\prime}
.

(c)

If
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\top
and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})=\bot
, then, by the definition of
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
, we have
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\top
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})=\bot
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\lor\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})\\

=\top
,
\llbracket a_{k}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})]}=\llbracket a_{k}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]}
and
\llbracket a_{k}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})]}=\llbracket a_{k}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}(t_{j}^{\prime})]}
,

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}&\models&(\neg\text{Del}(t_{i})\land\neg\text{Del}(t_{j}^{\prime}))\to(\neg\text{Del}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})\land\land_{k=1}^{p}\llbracket a_{k}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})]}=\llbracket a_{k}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i})]}\\
&&\qquad\land\land_{k=1}^{q}\llbracket a_{k}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})]}=\llbracket a_{k}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{j}^{\prime})]})\end{array}

and

\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\text{Del}(t_{i})\lor\text{Del}(t_{j}^{\prime})\to\text{Del}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})

Thus,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\Phi_{i,j}^{\prime\prime}
.

(d)

If
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\bot
and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})=\bot
, then, by the definition of
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
, we have
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\bot
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})=\bot
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\lor\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})\\

=\bot
,
\llbracket a_{k}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})]}=\llbracket a_{k}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]}
and
\llbracket a_{k}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})]}=\llbracket a_{k}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}(t_{j}^{\prime})]}
,

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}&\models&(\neg\text{Del}(t_{i})\land\neg\text{Del}(t_{j}^{\prime}))\to(\neg\text{Del}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})\land\land_{k=1}^{p}\llbracket a_{k}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})]}=\llbracket a_{k}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i})]}\\
&&\qquad\land\land_{k=1}^{q}\llbracket a_{k}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})]}=\llbracket a_{k}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime},[\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{j}^{\prime})]})\end{array}

and

\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\text{Del}(t_{i})\lor\text{Del}(t_{j}^{\prime})\to\text{Del}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})

Thus,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\Phi_{i,j}^{\prime\prime}
.

Thus,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\Phi\land\Phi^{\prime}\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\land_{j=1}^{m}\Phi_{i,j}^{\prime\prime}
and Theorem
4.5
for the inductive case
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\times Q^{\prime}
is proved.

(6)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\bowtie_{\phi}Q^{\prime}
.

This case can be proved by the inductive cases
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\times Q^{\prime}
and
Q^{\prime}=\sigma_{\phi}(Q)
.

(7)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q:[a_{1},\ldots,a_{p}]
,
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q^{\prime}:[a_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,a_{q}^{\prime}]
by Figure
10
;
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q\hookrightarrow R
where
R
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]
,
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q^{\prime}\hookrightarrow R^{\prime}
where
R^{\prime}
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,t_{m}^{\prime}]
by Figure
10
;
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\bowtie_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\leadsto\Phi
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\leadsto\Phi\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}^{\prime}
where
\Phi_{i}^{\prime}=\land_{k=1}^{p}\llbracket a_{k}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{i,m+1}^{\prime\prime}]}=\llbracket a_{k}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{i}]}\land\land_{k=1}^{q}\llbracket a_{k}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{i,m+1}^{\prime\prime}]}=\text{Null}\land(\neg\text{Del}(t_{i})\land\land_{j=1}^{m}\text{Del}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})\leftrightarrow\neg\text{Del}(t_{i,m+1}^{\prime\prime}))
by Figure
18
.

By the inductive hypothesis, we have
\mathcal{I}(R)=\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
,
\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime})=\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
and
\mathcal{I},\mathcal{D}\models\Phi
.

Take
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(t_{i})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})\leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{j}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(t_{j}^{\prime})
,

\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})=\textsf{merge}(t_{i},t_{j}^{\prime})
for
1\leq i\leq n
and
1\leq j\leq m
,

\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i,m+1}^{\prime\prime})=\textsf{merge}(t_{i},T_{\text{Null}})
for
1\leq i\leq n
,

\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\lor\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})\lor\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})]}
for
1\leq i\leq n
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i,m+1}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\lor\neg\land_{j=1}^{m}\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})
for
1\leq i\leq n
.

Let
x_{i}\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
,
x_{j}^{\prime}\in\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
where
1\leq i\leq c\leq n
,
1\leq j\leq d\leq m
,
c=|\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}|
and
d=|\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}|
, then

\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(x_{i})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(x_{j}^{\prime})=\bot
. Also, by the semantics of
foldl
and
append
, we know

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\llbracket Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}&=&\textsf{foldl}(\lambda xs.\lambda x.\textsf{append}(xs,\textsf{ite}(|v_{1}(x)|=0,v_{2}(x),v_{1}(x)),[],\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}))\\
&=&\textsf{foldl}(\lambda xs.\lambda x.\textsf{append}(xs,\textsf{ite}(|v_{1}(x)|=0,v_{2}(x),v_{1}(x)),[],\vec{x}_{1:c}))\\
&=&\textsf{foldl}(\lambda xs.\lambda x.\textsf{append}(xs,\textsf{ite}(|v_{1}(x)|=0,v_{2}(x),v_{1}(x)),\\
&&\quad[\textsf{ite}(|v_{1}(x_{1})|=0,v_{2}(x_{1}),v_{1}(x_{1}))],\vec{x}_{2:c}))\\
&=&\ldots\\
&=&[\\
&&\quad\textsf{ite}(|v_{1}(x_{1})|=0,v_{2}(x_{1}),v_{1}(x_{1}))\\
&&\quad\ldots\\
&&\quad\textsf{ite}(|v_{1}(x_{c})|=0,v_{2}(x_{c}),v_{1}(x_{c}))\\
&&]\end{array}

For any entry
\textsf{ite}(|v_{1}(x_{i})|=0,v_{2}(x_{i}),v_{1}(x_{i}))\in\llbracket Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\rrbracket
, let us discuss
|v_{1}(x_{i})|=0
in two cases.

(a)

If
|v_{1}(x_{i})|=0
is true, then
|\llbracket[x_{i}]\bowtie_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}|=0
and
\textsf{ite}(|v_{1}(x_{i})|=0,v_{2}(x_{i}),v_{1}(x_{i}))=v_{2}(x_{i})=[\textsf{merge}(x_{i},T_{\text{Null}})]
.
By the inductive case
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\times Q^{\prime}
, we know
\llbracket[x_{i}]\bowtie_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}=\sigma_{\phi}(\llbracket[x_{i}]\times Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}})=\sigma_{\phi}(\llbracket[x_{i}]\times[x_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,x_{d}^{\prime}]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}})=\sigma_{\phi}([x_{i,1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,x_{i,d}^{\prime\prime}])
where
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(x_{i})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(x_{j}^{\prime})=\bot
.
Therefore, for any
1\leq j\leq d
,
|\llbracket[x_{i}]\bowtie_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}|=0\Leftrightarrow\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[x_{i,j}^{\prime\prime}]}\neq\top
by Lemma
D.1
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(x_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})=\top
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(x_{i,c+1}^{\prime\prime})=\bot
,
Further, for
x_{i}\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
and
x_{j}^{\prime}\in\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
, there exist two symbolic tuples
t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}\in R
and
t_{\textsf{ptr}(j)}^{\prime}\in R^{\prime}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)})=x_{i}
,
\mathcal{I}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(j)}^{\prime})=x_{j}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(x_{i})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(x_{j}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(j)}^{\prime})=\bot
.
Therefore,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i),m+1}^{\prime\prime})=\textsf{merge}(x_{i},T_{\text{Null}})
and all tuples
t_{\textsf{ptr}(i),j}^{\prime\prime}
are deleted where
1\leq j\leq m
, i.e.,
\land_{j=1}^{m}\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i),j}^{\prime\prime})=\top
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i),m+1}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)})\lor\neg\land_{j=1}^{m}\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i),j}^{\prime\prime})=\bot
.

(b)

If
|v_{1}(x_{i})|=0
is false, then
|\llbracket[x_{i}]\bowtie_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}|\neq 0
and
\textsf{ite}(|v_{1}(x_{i})|=0,v_{2}(x_{i}),v_{1}(x_{i}))=v_{2}(x_{i})=[[x_{i,1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,x_{i,c}^{\prime\prime}]]
.
By the inductive case
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\times Q^{\prime}
, we know
\llbracket[x_{i}]\bowtie_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}=\sigma_{\phi}(\llbracket[x_{i}]\times Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}})=\sigma_{\phi}([x_{i,1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,x_{i,d}^{\prime\prime}])
.
Therefore,
|\llbracket[x_{i}]\bowtie_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}|\neq 0\Leftrightarrow\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[x_{i,j}^{\prime\prime}]}=\top
for some
1\leq j\leq d
by Lemma
D.1
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(x_{i,c+1}^{\prime\prime})=\top
.
Further, for
x_{i}\in\mathcal{I}(R)
and
x_{j}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime})
, there exist two symbolic tuples
t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}\in R
and
t_{\textsf{ptr}(j)}^{\prime}\in R^{\prime}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)})=x_{i}
,
\mathcal{I}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(j)}^{\prime})=x_{j}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(x_{i})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(j)}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(x_{j}^{\prime})=\bot
.
Therefore, we have
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i),m+1}^{\prime\prime})=\top
and some tuples
t_{\textsf{ptr}(i),j}^{\prime\prime}
remain alive, i.e.,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i),\textsf{ptr}(j)}^{\prime\prime})=\bot
for some
1\leq j\leq m
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i),\textsf{ptr}(j)}^{\prime\prime})=x_{i,j}^{\prime\prime}
.

Besides
7(a)
and
7(b)
, we know, for any tuple
t\in R^{\prime\prime}-\llbracket Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t)=\top
. Thus,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(R^{\prime\prime})=\llbracket Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
.

Let
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}=\mathcal{D}[o_{Q^{\prime\prime}}\mapsto\llbracket Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}]
.

T_{\text{Null}}(A)
denote a tuple that has the attributes from
A
but all their values are
Null
. For instance, assume
A=[\text{"a"},\text{"b"}]
, then
T_{\text{Null}}(A)=[(\text{"a"},\text{Null}),

(\text{"b"},\text{Null})]
.
By the semantics of
merge
and the definition of
T_{\text{Null}}
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\Phi_{j}^{\prime}
and, therefore,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\Phi\land\land_{j=1}^{m}\Phi_{j}^{\prime}

Hence, Theorem
4.5
for the inductive case
Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}
is proved.

(8)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\mathbin{\bowtie\mkern-5.8mu\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}
.

Following a similar method in the inductive case
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}
, we can find a
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie}_{\phi}Q^{\prime})=\llbracket Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
and a
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\Phi
.

(9)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie\mkern-5.8mu\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}
.

Suppose that by
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q:[a_{1},\ldots,a_{p}]
,
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q:[a_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,a_{q}^{\prime}]
by Figure
10
;
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q\hookrightarrow R
where
R
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]
,
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q^{\prime}\hookrightarrow R^{\prime}
where
R^{\prime}
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,t_{m}^{\prime}]
,
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie\mkern-5.8mu\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\hookrightarrow R^{\prime\prime}
where
R
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1,1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{n,m}^{\prime\prime},t_{n+1,1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{n+1,m}^{\prime\prime},t_{1,m+1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{n,m+1}^{\prime\prime}]
, by Figure
11
;
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\leadsto\Phi
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie\mkern-5.8mu\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\leadsto\Phi\land\land_{j=1}^{m}\Phi_{j}^{\prime}
where
\Phi_{j}^{\prime}=\land_{k=1}^{p}\llbracket a_{k}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{n+1,j}^{\prime\prime}]}=\text{Null}\land\land_{k=1}^{q}\llbracket a_{k}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{n+1,j}^{\prime\prime}]}=\llbracket a_{k}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{j}^{\prime}]}\land(\neg\text{Del}(t_{j}^{\prime})\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\text{Del}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})\leftrightarrow\neg\text{Del}(t_{n+1,j}^{\prime\prime}))
by Figure
18
.

By the inductive hypothesis, we have
\mathcal{I},\mathcal{D}\models\Phi
,
\mathcal{I}(R)=\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
,
\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime})=\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
,
\mathcal{I}([t_{1,1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,

t_{n,m}^{\prime\prime},t_{n+1,1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{n+1,m}^{\prime\prime}])=\llbracket Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
.

Take
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(t_{i})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})\leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(t_{i})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})\leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})
for
1\leq i\leq n
and
1\leq j\leq m+1
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{I}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})
for
1\leq i\leq n
and
1\leq j\leq m+1
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{n+1,j}^{\prime\prime})\leftrightarrow(\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})\lor\neg\land_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime}))
for
1\leq j\leq m
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{n+1,j}^{\prime\prime})=\textsf{merge}(T_{\text{Null}},t_{j}^{\prime})
for
1\leq j\leq m
.

Let
x_{j}^{\prime}\in\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
where
1\leq j\leq c\leq m
and
c=|\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}|
, then we have
x_{j}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}(Q^{\prime})
because
\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime})=\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
,
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(x_{j}^{\prime})=\bot
.
For any tuple
x_{j}^{\prime}\in\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
, we have a symbolic tuple
t_{\textsf{ptr}(j)}^{\prime}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(j)}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(x_{j}^{\prime})
and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(j)}^{\prime})=\bot
by the definition of
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
.
By the semantics of
filter
, we know
|v|\leq|\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}|
.
Also, for any tuple
x_{j}^{\prime}\in\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
,
\textsf{merge}(T_{\text{Null}},x_{j}^{\prime})
exists iff
|\llbracket Q\bowtie_{\phi}[x_{j}^{\prime}]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}|=0
.
Therefore,
\textsf{map}(v,\lambda x.\textsf{merge}(T_{\text{Null}},x))=\mathcal{I}^{\prime}([t_{n+1,1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{n+1,m}^{\prime\prime}])
by Figure
18
.
Furthermore, we have

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\llbracket Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie\mkern-5.8mu\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}&=&\textsf{append}(\llbracket Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}},\textsf{map}(v,\lambda x.\textsf{merge}(T_{\text{Null}},x)))\\
&=&\textsf{append}(\mathcal{I}^{\prime}([t_{1,1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{n,m+1}^{\prime\prime}]),\mathcal{I}^{\prime}([t_{n+1,1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{n+1,m}^{\prime\prime}]))\\
&=&\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(R^{\prime\prime})\end{array}

by the semantics of
map
.

Let
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}=\mathcal{D}[o_{Q^{\prime\prime}}\mapsto\llbracket Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie\mkern-5.8mu\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}]
.
By the semantics of
merge
and the definition of
T_{\text{Null}}
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\Phi_{j}^{\prime}
and, therefore,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\Phi\land\land_{j=1}^{m}\Phi_{j}^{\prime}

Hence, Theorem
4.5
for the inductive case
Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie\mkern-5.8mu\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}
is proved.

(10)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime}=\text{Distinct}(Q)
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\hookrightarrow R
where
R
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash\text{Distinct}(Q)\hookrightarrow R^{\prime}
where
R^{\prime}
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,t_{n}^{\prime}]
by Figure
11
;
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\leadsto\Phi
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\leadsto\Phi\land\Phi^{\prime}
where
\Phi^{\prime}=\text{Dedup}(\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}})
by Figure
18
.

By the inductive hypothesis, we have
\mathcal{I},\mathcal{D}\models\Phi
and
\mathcal{I}(R)=\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
.

Take
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{1})\leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{1})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})\\

(t_{i}^{\prime})\leftrightarrow\land_{j=1}^{i-1}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(t_{j})\land\neg\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}))
for
2\leq j\leq i
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(t_{i})
.

For any tuples
x_{i}^{\prime}
and
x_{j}^{\prime}
in
\llbracket\text{Distinct}(Q)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
s.t.
x_{i}^{\prime}\neq x_{j}^{\prime}
, we have two symbolic tuples
t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime}
and
t_{\textsf{ptr}(j)}^{\prime}
in
R
s.t.
x_{i}^{\prime}=\mathcal{I}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime})
and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime})=\bot
,
x_{j}^{\prime}=\mathcal{I}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(j)}^{\prime})
and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(j)}^{\prime})=\bot
.
Moreover, if
t_{i}
is a unique symbolic tuple in
R
, then
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(t_{i})
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\bot
where
t_{i}^{\prime}\in R^{\prime}
by the definition of
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
; otherwise, there exist some tuples
[t_{i_{1}},\ldots,t_{i_{l}}]
s.t.
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i_{j}})=\bot
and
t_{i_{j}}\in R
for
1\leq j\leq k
, and
\mathcal{I}(t_{i_{j}})=\mathcal{I}(t_{i_{k}})
for
1\leq j\neq k\leq k
.
Further, by the definition of
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime})=\land_{j=1}^{i}\mathcal{I}(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(t_{j})
, only the first symbolic tuple
t_{i_{1}}
will be retained in
R^{\prime}
and the other tuples
[t_{i_{2}},\ldots,t_{i_{l}}]
will be removed, i.e.,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i_{1}})=\top
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i_{j}})=\top
for
2\leq j\leq l
.
Therefore,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(R^{\prime})=\llbracket\text{Distinct}(Q)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
.

Let
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}=\mathcal{D}[o_{Q^{\prime}}\mapsto\text{Distinct}(Q)]
.
By the definition of
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
, we know
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models t_{1}^{\prime}=t_{1}\land\land_{i=2}^{n}(t_{i}\in\vec{t}_{1:i-1}\to\text{Del}(t_{i}^{\prime})\land t_{i}\not\in\vec{t}_{1:i-1}\to t_{i}^{\prime}=t_{i})
and, therefore,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\Phi\land\Phi^{\prime}
.

Hence, Theorem
4.5
for the inductive case
\text{Distinct}(Q)
is proved.

(11)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\cap Q^{\prime}
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{S}\vdash\text{Distinct}(Q)\hookrightarrow R
where
R
ash symbolic tuples
[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]
,
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q^{\prime}\hookrightarrow R^{\prime}
where
R^{\prime}
ash symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,t_{m}^{\prime}]
,
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q\cap Q^{\prime}\hookrightarrow R^{\prime\prime}
where
R^{\prime\prime}
ash symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{n}^{\prime\prime}]
by Figure
11
;
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash\text{Distinct}(Q)\leadsto\Phi
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q^{\prime\prime}\leadsto\Phi^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\cap Q^{\prime}\leadsto\Phi\land\Phi^{\prime}\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}^{\prime\prime}
where
\Phi_{i}^{\prime\prime}=t_{i}\in\vec{t^{\prime}}\to t_{i}^{\prime\prime}=t_{i}\land t_{i}\not\in\vec{t^{\prime}}\to\text{Del}(t_{i}^{\prime\prime})
by Figure
18
.

By the inductive hypothesis, we know
\mathcal{I},\mathcal{D}\models\Phi\land\Phi^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{I}(R)=\llbracket\text{Distinct}(Q)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
and
\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime})=\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
.

Take
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(t_{i})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{j}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{j})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})\leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{j})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{I}(t_{i})
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime\prime})\leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\lor\neg\lor_{j=1}^{m}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(t_{j}^{\prime})\land\neg\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime}))
.

For any tuple
x_{i}\in\llbracket\text{Distinct}(Q)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
, let us discuss
x_{i}\in\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
in two cases.

(a)

If
x_{i}\in\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
is true, then we can find at least one tuple
x_{j}^{\prime}\in\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
and its corresponding symbolic tuple
t_{\textsf{ptr}(j)}^{\prime}\in R^{\prime}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(x_{i})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(x_{j}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(j)}^{\prime})=\bot
and
\mathcal{I}(x_{i})=\mathcal{I}(x_{j}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(j)}^{\prime})
.
By the definition of
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
and the semantics of
filter
, we know there exist two symbolic tuples
t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime\prime}\in R^{\prime\prime}
and
t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}\in R
s.t.
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)})=\bot
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{I}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)})=\mathcal{I}(x_{i})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)})\lor\neg\lor_{j=1}^{m}(\mathcal{I}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)})=\mathcal{I}(t_{j}^{\prime})\land\neg\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime}))=\bot
.

(b)

If
x_{i}\in\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
is false, then
x_{i}
will be removed by the semantics of
filter
.
Assume the symbolic tuple
t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}\in R
is corresponding to
x_{i}
, i.e.,
\mathcal{I}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)})=\mathcal{I}(x_{i})
and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(x_{i})=\bot
, then
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\lor\neg\lor_{j=1}^{m}(\mathcal{I}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)})=\mathcal{I}(t_{j}^{\prime})\land\neg\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})\\

(t_{j}^{\prime}))=\top
.

By
11(a)
and
11(b)
, we have two symbolic tuples
t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}\in R
and
t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime\prime}\in R^{\prime\prime}
for any tuple
x_{i}\in\llbracket Q\cap Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)})=\bot
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)})
iff
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)})\lor\neg\lor_{j=1}^{m}(\mathcal{I}(t_{a})=\mathcal{I}(t_{j}^{\prime})\land\neg\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime}))
is false.
For the other tuple
t^{\prime\prime}\in R^{\prime\prime}-\llbracket Q\cap Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t^{\prime\prime})=\top
because
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t)=\top
or
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t)\not\in\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
.
Therefore,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(R^{\prime\prime})=\llbracket Q\cap Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
.

Let
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}=\mathcal{D}[o_{Q^{\prime\prime}}\mapsto\llbracket Q\cap Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}]
.
By the definition of
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models t_{i}\in\vec{t^{\prime}}\to t_{i}^{\prime\prime}=t_{i}\land t_{i}\not\in\vec{t^{\prime}}\to\text{Del}(t_{i}^{\prime\prime})
and, therefore,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\Phi\land\Phi^{\prime}\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\Phi^{\prime\prime}_{i}

Hence, Theorem
4.5
for the inductive case
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\cap Q^{\prime}
is proved.

(12)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\setminus Q^{\prime}
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{S}\vdash\text{Distinct}(Q)\hookrightarrow R
where

[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]

by Figure

\mathcal{I}(R)=\llbracket\text{Distinct}(Q)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
,
\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime})=\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
and
\mathcal{I},\mathcal{D}\models\Phi\land\Phi^{\prime}
.

Take
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(t_{i})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{j}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{j})
,

,

and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime\prime})\leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\lor\lor_{j=1}^{m}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(t_{j}^{\prime})\land\neg\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})\\

(t_{j}^{\prime}))

For any tuple
x_{i}\in\llbracket\text{Distinct}(Q)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}

x_{i}\in\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
in two cases.

x_{i}\in\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}

x_{i}
will be retained by the semantics of
filter
.

is corresponding to
x_{i}
, i.e.,
\mathcal{I}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)})=\mathcal{I}(x_{i})
and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{a})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(x_{i})
, then
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime\prime})=\neg\mathcal{I}(t_{i})\land\neg\lor_{j=1}^{m}(\mathcal{I}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)})=\mathcal{I}(t_{j}^{\prime})\land\neg\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime}))=\bot
.

By
12(a)
and
12(b)
, we have two symbolic tuples
t_{i}\in R
and
t_{i}^{\prime\prime}\in R^{\prime\prime}
for any tuple
x\in\llbracket Q\setminus Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\bot
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i})
iff
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\lor\lor_{j=1}^{m}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(t_{j}^{\prime})\land\neg\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime}))

,

and, therefore,

for the inductive case

(13)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q-Q^{\prime}
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q\hookrightarrow R
where
R
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]
,
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q^{\prime}\hookrightarrow R^{\prime}
where
R^{\prime}
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,t_{m}^{\prime}]
,
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q-Q^{\prime}\hookrightarrow R^{\prime\prime}
where
R^{\prime\prime}
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{n}^{\prime\prime}]
by Figure
11
;
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\leadsto\Phi
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q^{\prime}\leadsto\Phi^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q-Q^{\prime}\leadsto\Phi\land\Phi^{\prime}\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\land_{j=1}^{m}\Phi_{i,j}^{\prime\prime}
where
\Phi_{i,j}^{\prime\prime}=\neg\text{Del}(t_{i})\land\neg\text{Paired}(i,j)\to t_{i}^{\prime\prime}=t_{i}\land\text{Del}(t_{i})\lor\text{Paired}(i,j)\to\text{Del}(t_{i}^{\prime\prime})
by Figure
19
.

Let the Paired function only register in
\mathcal{I}
.
16
16

16

We slightly abuse interpretation here since the Paired function only works in this case.

Take
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(t_{i})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{j}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(t_{j}^{\prime})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})\leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{I}(t_{i})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime\prime})\leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\lor\lor_{j=1}^{m}\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Paired}(i,j))
.

By definition of
\llbracket Q-Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
, let
xs_{k}=\textsf{ite}(x^{\prime}\in xs_{k-1},xs_{k-1}-x,xs_{k-1})
and
xs_{0}=\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
.
Obviously, the value of
xs_{k}
will be updated iff
x_{j}^{\prime}\in xs_{k-1}
.
Let us discuss the predicate
x_{j}^{\prime}\in xs_{k-1}
in two cases.

(a)

If the predicate
x_{j}^{\prime}\in xs_{k-1}
is true, then we have two corresponding symbolic tuples
t_{i}\in R
and
t_{\textsf{ptr}(j)}^{\prime}\in R^{\prime}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(j)}^{\prime})=x_{j}^{\prime}\in xs_{k-1}
and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(j)}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(x_{j}^{\prime})=\bot
.
Therefore,
\text{Paired}(i,j)=\top
and
\text{Paired}(i,l)=\text{Paired}(k,j)=\bot
for any
1\leq k\neq i\leq n
and
1\leq l\neq j\leq m
to follow the semantics of
EXCEPT

ALL
.
Further, we know
xs_{k}=xs_{k-1}-x
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime\prime})=\top
.

(b)

If the predicate
x_{j}^{\prime}\in xs_{k-1}
is false, then we have a corresponding symbolic tuple
t_{\textsf{ptr}(j)}^{\prime}\in R^{\prime}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(j)}^{\prime})=x_{j}^{\prime}\not\in xs_{k-1}
,
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(j)}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(x_{j})=\bot
.
Also, by the definition of the Paired function, we know
\text{Paired}(i,{\textsf{ptr}(j)})=\bot
where
1\leq i\leq n
because
t_{\textsf{ptr}(j)}^{\prime}
is never paired with previously paired tuples in
R
and
\mathcal{I}(x_{i})\neq\mathcal{I}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(j)}^{\prime})
.
Therefore, we have
xs_{k}=xs_{k-1}
.

Let
xs^{\prime}=[x_{i_{1}},\ldots,x_{i_{l}}]
be the deleted tuples from
\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
,
i_{k}
the real index in
\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
and
1\leq k\leq l
.
By
13(a)
and
13(b)
, we have corresponding symbolic tuples
t_{\textsf{ptr}(i_{k})}^{\prime\prime}\in R^{\prime\prime}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i_{k})}^{\prime\prime})=\top
.
Further, for any symbolic tuple
t_{i}\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}-xs^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime\prime})=\bot
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{I}(t_{i})
; for the resting symbolic tuples
t\in R^{\prime}-\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t^{\prime\prime})=\top
because
t\not\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
.
Therefore,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(R^{\prime\prime})=\llbracket Q-Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
.

Let
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}=\mathcal{D}[o_{Q^{\prime\prime}}\mapsto\llbracket Q-Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}]

For any tuple
xs_{i}^{\prime}\in xs^{\prime}
, there exist symbolic tuples
t_{a}\in R
and
t_{b}^{\prime}\in R^{\prime}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}(xs_{i}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(t_{a})=\mathcal{I}(t_{b}^{\prime})
,
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(xs_{i}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{a})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{b}^{\prime})=\bot
and
\text{Paired}(a,b)=\top
.
Also, by the definition of the Paired function, we know
\text{Paired}(a,j)=\bot
for any
j\neq b
and
\text{Paired}(i,b)=\bot
for any
i\neq a
.
Therefore,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}\models\neg\text{Del}(t_{a})\land\land_{j=1}^{m}\neg\text{Paired}(a,j)\to t_{a}^{\prime\prime}=t_{a}\land\text{Del}(t_{a})\lor\lor_{j=1}^{m}\neg\text{Paired}(a,j)\to\text{Del}(t_{a}^{\prime\prime})
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\Phi\land\Phi^{\prime}\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\land_{j=1}^{m}\Phi_{i,j}^{\prime\prime}

Hence, Theorem
4.5
for the inductive case
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q-Q^{\prime}
is proved.

(14)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\cup Q^{\prime}
.

Since
Q\cup Q^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow\text{Distinct}(Q\mathrel{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\uplus$}}}Q^{\prime})
, this case can be proved by the inductive cases
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\mathrel{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\uplus$}}}Q^{\prime}
and
Q^{\prime}=\text{Distinct}(Q\mathrel{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\uplus$}}}Q^{\prime})
.

(15)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\uplus Q^{\prime}
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q\hookrightarrow R
where
R
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]
,
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q^{\prime}\hookrightarrow R^{\prime}
where
R^{\prime}
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,t_{m}^{\prime}]
,
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q\uplus Q^{\prime}\hookrightarrow R^{\prime\prime}
where
R^{\prime\prime}
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{n+m}^{\prime\prime}]
by Figure
11
;
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\leadsto\Phi
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q^{\prime}\leadsto\Phi^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\uplus Q^{\prime}\leadsto\Phi\land\Phi^{\prime}\land\Phi^{\prime\prime}
where
\Phi^{\prime\prime}=\land_{i=1}^{n}t_{i}^{\prime\prime}=t_{i}\land\land_{j=n+1}^{n+m}t_{j}^{\prime\prime}=t_{j-n}^{\prime}
by Figure
18
.

By the inductive hypothesis, we know
\mathcal{I}(R)=\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
,
\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime})=\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
and
\mathcal{I},\mathcal{D}\models\Phi\land\Phi^{\prime}
.

Take
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(t_{i})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{j}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(t_{j}^{\prime})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})\leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{I}(t_{i})
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime\prime})\leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i})
for
1\leq i\leq n
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{j}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{I}(t_{j-n}^{\prime})
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime\prime})\leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})
for
n+1\leq i\leq n+m
.

Further, since
\mathcal{I}(R)=\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
and
\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime})=\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
,

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\llbracket Q\uplus Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}&=&\textsf{append}(\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}},\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}})\\
&=&\textsf{append}(\mathcal{I}(R),\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime}))\\
&=&\textsf{append}(\mathcal{I}([t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]),\mathcal{I}([t_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,t_{m}^{\prime}]))\\
&=&\textsf{append}([\mathcal{I}(t_{1}),\ldots,\mathcal{I}(t_{n})],[\mathcal{I}(t_{1}^{\prime}),\ldots,\mathcal{I}(t_{m}^{\prime})])\\
&=&[\mathcal{I}(t_{1}),\ldots,\mathcal{I}(t_{n}),\mathcal{I}(t_{1}^{\prime}),\ldots,\mathcal{I}(t_{m}^{\prime})]\\
&=&[\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{1}^{\prime\prime}),\ldots,\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{n}^{\prime\prime}),\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{n+1}^{\prime\prime}),\ldots,\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{n+m}^{\prime\prime})]\\
&=&\mathcal{I}^{\prime}([t_{1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{n}^{\prime\prime},t_{n+1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{n+m}^{\prime\prime}])\\
&=&\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(R^{\prime\prime})\end{array}

Let
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}=\mathcal{D}[o_{Q^{\prime\prime}}\mapsto\llbracket Q\uplus Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}]
.
By the definition of
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
, we have
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\land_{i=1}^{n}t_{i}^{\prime\prime}=t_{i}\land\land_{j=n+1}^{n+m}t_{j}^{\prime\prime}=t_{j-n}^{\prime}
and, therefore,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\Phi\land\Phi^{\prime}\land\Phi^{\prime\prime}
.

Hence, Theorem
4.5
for the inductive case
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\uplus Q^{\prime}
is proved.

(16)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\mathrel{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\uplus$}}}Q^{\prime}
.

Since
Q\mathrel{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\uplus$}}}Q^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow Q-(Q-Q^{\prime})
, this case can be proved by the inductive case
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q-Q^{\prime}
.

(17)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime}=\text{GroupBy}(Q,\vec{E},L,\phi)
.

Suppose that
\Gamma\vdash Q\hookrightarrow R
where
R
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]
,
\Gamma\vdash\text{GroupBy}(Q,\vec{E},L,\phi)\hookrightarrow R
where
R
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,t_{n}^{\prime}]
by Figure
11
;
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\leadsto\Phi
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash\text{GroupBy}(Q,\vec{E},

L,\phi)\leadsto\Phi\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}^{\prime}\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}^{\prime\prime}
where
\Phi_{i}^{\prime}=\Sigma_{j=1}^{n}\text{If}(g(t_{i},j),0,1)=\text{If}(\text{Del}(t_{i}),0,1)\land\land_{j=1}^{i-1}g(t_{i},j)=(\neg\text{Del}(t_{i})\land g(t_{i},j)\land\llbracket\vec{E}\rrbracket)_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{i}]}=\llbracket\vec{E}\rrbracket)_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{i}j}
and
\Phi_{i}^{\prime\prime}=(g(t_{i},j)\land\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,g^{-1}(i)}=\top\to\neg\text{Del}(t_{i}^{\prime})\land\land_{k=1}^{l}\llbracket a_{k}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{i}^{\prime}]}=\llbracket a_{k}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,g^{-1}(i)})\land(\neg g(t_{i},j)\lor\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,g^{-1}(i)}\neq\top\to\text{Del}(t_{i}^{\prime}))
by Figure
18
;
L=[a_{1},\ldots a_{l}]
.

By the inductive hypothesis, we have
\mathcal{I}(R)=\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
and
\mathcal{I},\mathcal{D}\models\Phi
.

Take
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(t_{i})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime})\leftrightarrow\neg g(t_{i},i)
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i})=\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[g^{-1}(i)]}
where
g
checks whether
t_{i}
is partitioned into the
i
-th group, and
g^{-1}(i)
finds symbolic tuples from the
i
-th group.

For any tuple
x_{i}\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
, we have a symbolic tuple
t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime}\in R
s.t.
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(x_{i})\\

=\bot
and
\mathcal{I}(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(x_{i})
.
By the definition of
foldl
, we know
\text{Dedup}(Q,\vec{E})=\\

\text{Distinct}([\llbracket\vec{E}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[x_{1}]},\ldots,\llbracket\vec{E}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[x_{c}]}])
where
c=|\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}|\leq n
.
Assuming that
\text{Dedup}(Q,\vec{E})=[\llbracket\vec{E}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[x_{f(1)}]},\ldots,\llbracket\vec{E}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},[x_{f(d)}]}]
after executing Distinct operation where
d=|\text{Dedup}(Q,\vec{E})|

\leq c
and the function
f(i)
is the original index for the
i
-th entry in
\text{Dedup}(Q,\vec{E})
.
Furthermore, by the definition of
ys=\textsf{map}(\text{Dedup}(Q,\vec{E}),\lambda y.\textsf{map}(\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}},\lambda z.\text{Eval}(\vec{E},[z])=y))
, we have a set of tuples
G_{i}
sharing the same values over
\vec{E}
for any entry
e_{f(i)}\in\text{Dedup}(Q,\vec{E})
.

On the other hand, the definition of
g(t_{i},j)
guarantees two properties. The first property states that, for any existing symbolic tuple
t_{i}
, it only can be partitioned into one symbolic group
G_{j}^{\prime}
whose group index
j
cannot be greater than
i
, i.e.,
\sum_{j=1}^{i}\text{If}(g(t_{i},j),1,0)=\text{If}(\text{Del}(t_{i}),0,1)
; otherwise, this deleted symbolic tuple
t_{i}
never belongs to any group.
The second one ensures the symbolic tuple
t_{i}
is partitioned into a previous symbolic group
G_{j}^{\prime}
iff
t_{i}
and
t_{j}\in G_{j}^{\prime}
shares the same values over
\vec{E}
, i.e.,
\land_{j=1}^{i-1}g(t_{i},j)=(\neg\text{Del}(t_{i})\land g(t_{i},j)\land\llbracket\vec{E}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{i}]}=\llbracket\vec{E}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{j}]})
.
Therefore, we have symbolic groups
[G_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,G_{n}^{\prime}]
as every tuple might be not deleted and unique over
\vec{E}
.

Let us discuss the tuple
x_{i}\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
in the two cases.

(a)

If no other tuple
x_{j}\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
shares the same values over
\vec{E}
with
x_{i}
, then only
x_{i}
will be partitioned into a group
G_{i}
. Similarly, the symbolic tuple
t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}
is separated into the symbolic group
G_{i}^{\prime}
because
g(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)},i)=\top
and
g(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)},j)=\top
where
1\leq j\leq i-1
.

(b)

If there exist some tuples in
\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
share the same values over
\vec{E}
with
x_{i}
.
Let
x_{j}
be the first tuple in the group
G_{j}
, then we have
\llbracket E\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},t_{j}}=\llbracket E\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},t_{i}}
where
j<i
and
G_{j}=\textsf{append}(G_{j},t_{i})
.
In symbolic setting, we have
g(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)},t_{\textsf{ptr}(j)})=\top
because
g(t_{j},j)=\top
and
\llbracket\vec{E}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{i}]}=\llbracket\vec{E}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{j}]}
is true.

By
17(a)
and
17(b)
, we know if
\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
is partitioned into
[G_{1},\ldots,G_{m}]
where
1\leq m\leq|\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}|
, there exists a symbolic group list s.t.
[G_{\textsf{ptr}(1)}^{\prime},\ldots,G_{\textsf{ptr}(m)}^{\prime}]
and
G_{i}=\mathcal{I}(G_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime})
for
1\leq i\leq m
because
\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}=\mathcal{I}(R)
.
For any other group
G_{j}^{\prime}\in[G_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,G_{n}^{\prime}]-[G_{\textsf{ptr}(1)}^{\prime},\ldots,G_{\textsf{ptr}(m)}^{\prime}]
, there is no symbolic tuple inside, i.e.,
\mathcal{I}(g^{-1}(j))=[]
by the definition of the function
g(t_{i},j)
.
Therefore,
[G_{1},\ldots,G_{m}]=\mathcal{I}([G_{\textsf{ptr}(1)}^{\prime},\ldots,G_{\textsf{ptr}(m)}^{\prime}])
.
Furthermore, since we will perform the same operations (e.g.,
\sigma_{\phi}
and
\Pi
) over
[G_{1},\ldots,G_{m}]
and
[G_{\textsf{ptr}(1)}^{\prime},\ldots,G_{\textsf{ptr}(m)}^{\prime}]
,

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\llbracket\text{GroupBy}(Q,\vec{E},L,\phi)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}&=&\textsf{map}(\textsf{filter}([G_{1},\ldots,G_{m}],\lambda xs.\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},xs}=\top),\lambda xs.\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},xs})\\
&=&\textsf{map}(\textsf{filter}(\mathcal{I}([G_{\textsf{ptr}(1)}^{\prime},\ldots,G_{\textsf{ptr}(m)}^{\prime}]),\lambda xs.\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},xs}=\top),\lambda xs.\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},xs})\\
&=&\mathcal{I}(\textsf{map}(\textsf{filter}([G_{\textsf{ptr}(1)}^{\prime},\ldots,G_{\textsf{ptr}(m)}^{\prime}],\lambda xs.\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},xs}=\top),\lambda xs.\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},xs}))\\
&=&\mathcal{I}(\text{GroupBy}(Q,\vec{E},L,\phi))\\
\end{array}

Let
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}=\mathcal{D}[Q^{\prime}\mapsto\llbracket\text{GroupBy}(Q,\vec{E},L,\phi)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}]
, then we know
x_{i}^{\prime}=\textsf{map}(\textsf{filter}([G_{i}],\lambda xs.\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},xs}

=\top),\lambda xs.\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},xs})
and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(x_{i})

=\bot
for any tuple
x_{i}^{\prime}\in\llbracket\text{GroupBy}(Q,\vec{E},L,\phi)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
.

If
x_{i}
is the first tuple of
G_{i}
, then
t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}
is the first symbolic tuple of
G_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime}
, and
g^{-1}(\textsf{ptr}(i))=G_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime}
.
Since
ys\in[G_{1},\ldots,G_{m}]
, let us discuss the predicate
\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},G_{i}}
in two cases.

(a)

If
\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},G_{i}}\neq\top
is true, then the group
G_{i}
will be removed and the tuple
\textsf{map}(\textsf{filter}([G_{i}],

\lambda xs.\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},xs}=\top),\lambda xs.\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},xs})
does not exist,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime})=\top
,
\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},\mathcal{I}(G_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime})}=\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},\mathcal{I}(g^{-1}(\textsf{ptr}(i)))}\neq\top
.
As
g(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)},i)
always holds for
G_{i}
, we know
g(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)},i)\land\\

\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},\mathcal{I}(g^{-1}(\textsf{ptr}(i)))}=\top
is false and
\neg g(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)},i)\lor\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},\mathcal{I}(g^{-1}(\textsf{ptr}(i)))}\neq\top
is true.
Therefore,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\Phi_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime\prime}
.

(b)

If
\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},G_{i}}\neq\top
is false, then the group
G_{i}
will projected out and the tuple
\textsf{map}(\textsf{filter}([G_{i}],

\lambda xs.\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},xs}=\top),\lambda xs.\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},xs})
does exist,
\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},\mathcal{I}(G_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime})}=\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},\mathcal{I}(g^{-1}(\textsf{ptr}(i)))}=\top
,

\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime})=\bot
.
As
g(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)},i)
always holds for
G_{i}
, we know
g(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)},i)\land\\

\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},\mathcal{I}(g^{-1}(\textsf{ptr}(i)))}=\top
is true and
