\neg g(t_{\textsf{ptr}(i)},i)\lor\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},\mathcal{I}(g^{-1}(\textsf{ptr}(i)))}\neq\top
is false.
Therefore,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\Phi_{\textsf{ptr}(i)}^{\prime\prime}
.

Also, by the definition of the function
g
, we know
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\land_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}^{\prime}
.

Hence,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\Phi\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}^{\prime}\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}^{\prime\prime}
and Theorem
4.5
for the inductive case
Q^{\prime}=\text{GroupBy}(Q,

\vec{E},L,\phi)
is proved.

(18)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime\prime}=\text{With}(\vec{Q},\vec{T},Q^{\prime})
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q_{i}:\mathcal{A}_{i}
by Figure
10
;
\Gamma\vdash Q_{i}\hookrightarrow R_{i}
by Figure
11
;
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q_{i}\leadsto\Phi_{i}
by Figure
18
.

By the inductive hypothesis, we have
\mathcal{I}(R_{i})=\llbracket Q_{i}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
where
1\leq i\leq n
and
n=|\vec{Q}|=|\vec{T}|
;

\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash Q^{\prime}\hookrightarrow R^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash Q^{\prime}\leadsto\Phi^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime})=\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}_{n}}
where
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}=\mathcal{S}[T_{1}\mapsto\mathcal{A}_{1},\ldots,T_{n}\mapsto\mathcal{A}_{n}]
and
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma[T_{1}\mapsto\mathcal{T}_{1},\ldots,T_{n}\mapsto\mathcal{T}_{n}]
.
Also, by Figure
17
, we know
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}=\mathcal{D}[R_{i}\mapsto\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}~|~R_{i}\in R]=[R_{1}\mapsto\llbracket Q_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}},\ldots,R_{n}\mapsto\llbracket Q_{n}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}]
.

Let
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}=\mathcal{I}
,
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}=\mathcal{D}^{\prime}[o_{Q^{\prime}}\mapsto\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}]
.
Obviously, we have
\llbracket\text{With}(\vec{Q},\vec{T},Q^{\prime})\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}}=\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}}=\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime})

and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}\models\land_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}\land\Phi^{\prime}
.

Hence, Theorem
4.5
for the inductive case
Q^{\prime\prime}=\text{With}(\vec{Q},\vec{T},Q^{\prime})
is proved.

(19)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime}=\text{OrderBy}(Q,\vec{E},b)
.

Suppose that
\Gamma\vdash Q\hookrightarrow R
where
R
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]
,
\Gamma\vdash\text{OrderBy}(Q,\vec{E},b)\hookrightarrow R^{\prime}
where
R^{\prime}
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,t_{n}^{\prime}]
Figure
11
;

\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\leadsto\Phi
by Figure
19
;

By the inductive hypothesis, we have
\mathcal{I}(R)=\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
and
\mathcal{I},\mathcal{D}\models\Phi
.

We create a fresh symbolic table
R^{\prime\prime}
where
R^{\prime\prime}
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{n}^{\prime\prime}]
, and the function
\text{moveDelToEnd}(R,R^{\prime\prime})
can ”remove” deleted symbolic tuples in
R
by moving them to the end of a list
R
and return the sorted list to
R^{\prime\prime}
.
Further,
\text{moveDelToEnd}(\vec{t},\vec{t}^{\prime\prime})
guarantees that there exists
m
symbolic tuples s.t.
\forall i\leq m.\text{Del}(t_{i})=\bot
and
\forall m<i.\text{Del}(t_{i})=\top
and, for each tuple
t_{i}^{\prime\prime}\in R^{\prime\prime}
, we can find its corresponding tuple
t_{\text{indexOf}(\vec{t},i)}
in
R
where the
\text{indexOf}(R,i)
function take as input a symbolic tuple list
R
and an index
i
, and returns the corresponding index
\text{indexOf}(R,i)
s.t.
\mathcal{I}(t_{i}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{I}(t_{\text{indexOf}(\vec{t},i)})
and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{\text{indexOf}(\vec{t},i)})
because
t_{i}^{\prime\prime}=t_{\text{indexOf}(\vec{t},i)}
.

Take
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(t_{i})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime\prime})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{I}(t_{\text{indexOf}(\vec{t},i)})
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(t_{\text{find}(i,\vec{E},b)}^{\prime\prime})
and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime})\leftrightarrow\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{\text{find}(i,\vec{E},b)}^{\prime\prime})
where the
find
function seeks out the
i
-th smallest symbolic tuple
t_{\text{find}(i,\vec{E},b)^{\prime\prime}}\in R^{\prime\prime}
w.r.t. the expression list
\vec{E}
and the ascending flag
b
.
Additionally, for those deleted tuples
[t_{m+1},\ldots t_{n}]
in
R^{\prime\prime}
, the
find
function will not consider them because they are ”deleted”.
Hence, for any tuple
t_{i}^{\prime\prime}\in[t_{1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{m}^{\prime\prime}]
, we always can find a tuple
t_{\text{find}(i,\vec{E},b)}^{\prime\prime}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{\text{find}(i,\vec{E},b)}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{\text{find}(\text{indexOf}(\vec{t},i),\vec{E},b)}^{\prime\prime})=\bot
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(t_{\text{find}(i,\vec{E},b)}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{I}(t_{\text{find}(\text{indexOf}(\vec{t},i)}^{\prime\prime},\vec{E},b))
.

To compare two tuples (e.g.,
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
) w.r.t. the expression list
\vec{E}
and the ascending flag
b
, let us discuss the value of
\text{Cmp}(\vec{E},b,x_{1},x_{2})
in three cases.

(a)

If
v(x_{1},E_{i})<v(x_{2},E_{i})
, then
\text{Cmp}(\vec{E},b,x_{1},x_{2})=b\neq\top
by the semantics of
foldr
.
Furthermore, if
b=\top
(i.e., in ascending order), then
\text{Cmp}(\vec{E},b,x_{1},x_{2})=\bot
and vice versa.

(b)

If
v(x_{1},E_{i})>v(x_{2},E_{i})
, then
\text{Cmp}(\vec{E},b,x_{1},x_{2})=b\neq\bot
by the semantics of
foldr
.
Furthermore, if
b=\top
(i.e., in ascending order), then
\text{Cmp}(\vec{E},b,x_{1},x_{2})=\top
and vice versa.

(c)

If
v(x_{1},E_{i})=v(x_{2},E_{i})
, then
\text{Cmp}(\vec{E},b,x_{1},x_{2})=b\neq\textsf{foldr}(\lambda E_{i}.\lambda y.\textsf{ite}(v(x_{1},E_{i})<v(x_{2},E_{i}),\\

\top,\textsf{ite}(v(x_{1},E_{i})>v(x_{2},E_{i}),\bot,y)),\top,\vec{E}_{i+1:m})
where
m=|\vec{E}|
,
y
denotes the accumulator of
foldr
and
\text{Cmp}(\vec{E},b,x_{1},x_{2})
will be determined by the following expressions
[E_{i+1},\ldots,E_{m}]
. Also, we initialize the accumulator
y
to
\top
which aims to find the first smallest (last biggest) tuple if
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
are equal w.r.t.
\vec{E}
and b.

Further, by the semantics of
foldl
and the value of
\text{Cmp}(\vec{E},b,x_{1},x_{2})
, we know
\text{MinTuple}(\vec{E},b,

xs)
is to find the first smallest (last biggest) tuple from
xs
w.r.t.
\vec{E}
and
b
.

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\text{MinTuple}(\vec{E},b,xs)&=&\textsf{foldl}(\lambda x.\lambda y.\textsf{ite}(\text{Cmp}(\vec{E},b,x_{1},x_{2}),y,x),\textsf{head}(xs),xs)\\
&=&\textsf{foldl}(\lambda x.\lambda y.\textsf{ite}(\text{Cmp}(\vec{E},b,x_{1},x_{2}),y,x),\\
&&\qquad\textsf{ite}(\text{Cmp}(\vec{E},b,xs_{1},xs_{1}),xs_{1},xs_{1}),xs_{2:n})\\
&=&\textsf{foldl}(\lambda x.\lambda y.\textsf{ite}(\text{Cmp}(\vec{E},b,x_{1},x_{2}),y,x),xs_{1},xs_{2:n})\\
\end{array}

Let us discuss
\text{MinTuple}(\vec{E},b,xs)
in two cases.

(a)

If
\text{Cmp}(\vec{E},b,x_{1},x_{2})
is true, the accumulator (i.e., the smallest or biggest tuple of
xs
) in
\text{MinTuple}(\vec{E},b,xs)
will be updated.

(b)

If
\text{Cmp}(\vec{E},b,x_{1},x_{2})
is false, the accumulator (i.e., the smallest or biggest tuple of
xs
) in
\text{MinTuple}(\vec{E},b,xs)
remains constant.

Therefore,
\text{MinTuple}(\vec{E},b,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}-xs)
seeks out the smallest (biggest) tuple from
\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}-xs
.
Let
a_{i}
denote the
i
-th smallest (biggest) tuple from
\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}-xs
(i.e.,
a_{i}=\text{MinTuple}(\vec{E},b,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}-xs)
where
xs=[a_{i},\ldots,a_{i-1}]
) and
\vec{t}_{1:p}=\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
, then

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\llbracket\text{OrderBy}(Q,\vec{E},b)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}&=&\textsf{foldl}(\lambda xs.\lambda\_.(\textsf{append}(xs,\text{MinTuple}(\vec{E},b,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}-xs))),[],\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}})\\
&=&\textsf{foldl}(\lambda xs.\lambda\_.(\textsf{append}(xs,\text{MinTuple}(\vec{E},b,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}-xs))),[],\vec{t}_{1:p})\\
&=&\textsf{foldl}(\lambda xs.\lambda\_.(\textsf{append}(xs,\text{MinTuple}(\vec{E},b,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}-xs))),[a_{1}],\vec{t}_{2:p})\\
&&\ldots\\
&=&[a_{1},\ldots,a_{p}]\\
\end{array}

Since (i)
t_{\text{find}(i,\vec{E},b)}^{\prime\prime}
and
a_{i}
are the
i
-th smallest (biggest) tuples from
\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime\prime})
and
\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
, respectively, (ii)
\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime\prime})=\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
, we have, for any
1\leq i\leq p
,
\mathcal{I}(t_{\text{find}(i,\vec{E},b)}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{I}(a_{i})
and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{\text{find}(i,\vec{E},b)}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(a_{i})=\bot
; and, for any
p\leq i\leq n
,
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{\text{find}(i,\vec{E},b)}^{\prime\prime})=\top
.
Also by the definition of
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
, we have
t_{i}^{\prime}=t_{\text{find}(i,\vec{E},b)}^{\prime\prime}
,
\mathcal{I}(t_{i}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(a_{i})
and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(a_{i})=\bot
where
1\leq i\leq p
.
Thus,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{OrderBy}(Q,\vec{E},b))=\llbracket\text{OrderBy}(Q,\vec{E},b)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}
.

Let
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}=\mathcal{D}[o_{\text{OrderBy}(Q,\vec{E},b)}\mapsto\llbracket\text{OrderBy}(Q,\vec{E},b)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D}}]
.
Obviously,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\models\Phi\land\Phi^{\prime}\land\Phi^{\prime\prime}
where
\Phi^{\prime}=\text{moveDelToEnd}(\vec{t},\vec{t}^{\prime\prime})
and
\Phi^{\prime\prime}=\land_{i=1}^{n}t_{i}^{\prime}=t_{\text{find}(i,\vec{E},b)}^{\prime\prime}
hold because
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{i}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{\text{find}(i,\vec{E},b)}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(t_{\text{find}(\text{indexOf}(\vec{t},i),\vec{E},b)})
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})(t_{\text{find}(i,\vec{E},b)})=\mathcal{I}^{\prime}(\text{Del})

(t_{\text{find}(\text{indexOf}(\vec{t},i),\vec{E},b)})
.

Hence, Theorem
4.5
for the inductive case
Q^{\prime}=\text{OrderBy}(Q,\vec{E},b)
is proved.

∎

Proof of Theorem
4.6
:

Let
\Gamma
be a symbolic database over schema
\mathcal{S}
and
Q
be a query. Consider a symbolic relation
R
and a formula
\Phi
such that
\Gamma\vdash Q\hookrightarrow R
and
\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\vdash Q\leadsto\Phi
. If
\Phi
is satisfiable, then for any satisfying interpretation
\mathcal{I}
of
\Phi
, running
Q
over the concrete database
\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)
yields the relation
\mathcal{I}(R)
, i.e.,

(\Gamma\vdash Q\hookrightarrow R)\land(\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\leadsto\Phi)\land(\mathcal{I}\models\Phi)\Rightarrow\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}=\mathcal{I}(R)

∎

Proof.

By structural induction on
Q
.

(1)

Base case:
Q=T
.

R=\Gamma(T)
by Figure
11
.

\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}=\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)(T)
by Figure
17
.
Therefore,
\llbracket T\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}=\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)(T)=\mathcal{I}(\Gamma(T))=\mathcal{I}(R)
.

Thus, Theorem
4.6
for the inductive case
Q=T
is proved.

(2)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime}=\Pi_{L}(Q)
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q:[a_{1},\ldots,a_{m}]
by Figure
10
;

\mathcal{S}\vdash\sigma_{L}(Q):[a_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,a_{l}^{\prime}]
where
l=|L|
by Figure
10
;

\Gamma\vdash Q\hookrightarrow R
where
R
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]
by Figure
11
;

\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\leadsto\Phi
by Figure
18
;

\llbracket\Pi_{L}(Q)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}=\textsf{ite}(\text{hasAggr}(L),[\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}}],\textsf{map}(\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)},\lambda x.\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),x}))
by Figure
17
.

Let us discuss
\text{hasAggr}(L)
in two cases.

(a)

If
\text{hasAggr}(L)
is true, then
\Gamma\vdash\Pi_{L}(Q)\hookrightarrow R^{\prime}
where
R^{\prime}
only has one symbolic tuple
[t_{1}^{\prime}]
by Figure
11
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash\Pi_{L}(Q)\leadsto\Phi\land\land_{i=1}^{l}\Phi_{i}^{\prime}\land(\text{Del}(t_{1}^{\prime})\leftrightarrow\land_{i=1}^{n}\text{Del}(t_{i}))
where
\Phi_{i}^{\prime}=\llbracket a_{j}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{1}^{\prime}]}=\llbracket a_{j}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\vec{t}}
by Figure
12
.

Take
\mathcal{I}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}\models\Phi\land\land_{i=1}^{l}\Phi_{i}^{\prime}\land\neg\text{Del}(t_{1}^{\prime})
.

By Lemma
D.5
, we know
\mathcal{I}(\llbracket A\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})=\llbracket A\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}
.
Also, by Figure
17
, we have

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\llbracket\Pi_{L}(Q)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}&=&[\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}}]=[\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}([t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}])}]\\
&=&[(\text{ToString}(a_{1}^{\prime}),\llbracket a_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}([t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}])}),\ldots,(\text{ToString}(a_{l}^{\prime}),\llbracket a_{l}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}([t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}])})]\\
&=&[(\text{ToString}(a_{1}^{\prime}),\llbracket a_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),[\mathcal{I}(t_{1}^{\prime})]}),\ldots,(\text{ToString}(a_{l}^{\prime}),\llbracket a_{l}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),[\mathcal{I}(t_{1}^{\prime})]})]\\
&=&[\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),[\mathcal{I}(t_{1}^{\prime})]}]=\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime})\end{array}

(b)

If
\text{hasAggr}(L)
is false, then
\Gamma\vdash\Pi_{L}(Q)\hookrightarrow R^{\prime}
where
R^{\prime}
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,t_{n}^{\prime}]
by Figure
11
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash\Pi_{L}(Q)\leadsto\Phi\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}^{\prime}
where
\Phi_{i}^{\prime}=\land_{j=1}^{l}\llbracket a_{j}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{i}^{\prime}]}=\llbracket a_{j}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{i}]}\land\text{Del}(t_{i}^{\prime})\leftrightarrow\text{Del}(t_{i})
by Figure
12
.

Take
\mathcal{I}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}\models\Phi\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}^{\prime}
.

By Figure
17
, we have

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\llbracket\Pi_{L}(Q)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}&=&\textsf{map}(\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)},\lambda x.\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),x})\\
&=&\textsf{map}(\mathcal{I}([t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]),\lambda x.\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),x})\\
&=&[\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),[\mathcal{I}(t_{1})]},\ldots,\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),[\mathcal{I}(t_{n})]}]\\
&=&[\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),[\mathcal{I}(t_{1}^{\prime})]},\ldots,\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),[\mathcal{I}(t_{n}^{\prime})]}]\\
&=&\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime})\end{array}

Thus,
\llbracket\Pi_{L}(Q)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}=\mathcal{I}(R)
by
2(a)
and
2(b)
and Theorem
4.6
for the inductive case
Q^{\prime}=\Pi_{L}(Q)
is proved.

(3)

Inductive case:
Q=\sigma_{\phi}(Q)
.

Suppose that
\Gamma\vdash Q\hookrightarrow R
where
R
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]
,
\Gamma\vdash\sigma_{\phi}(Q)\hookrightarrow R^{\prime}
where
R^{\prime}
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,t_{n}^{\prime}]
by Figure
11
;
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\leadsto\Phi\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}^{\prime}
where
\Phi_{i}^{\prime}=(\neg\text{Del}(t_{i})\land\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{i}]}=\top)\to t_{i}^{\prime}=t\land(\text{Del}(t_{i})\lor\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{i}]}\neq\top)\to\text{Del}(t_{i}^{\prime})
by Figure
18
.

Take
\mathcal{I}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}\models\Phi\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}^{\prime}
.

By Lemma
D.1
, we know
\mathcal{I}(\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[x]})=\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}([x])}
and, therefore,

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\llbracket\sigma_{\phi}(Q)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}&=&\textsf{filter}(\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)},\lambda x.\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),[x]}=\top)\\
&=&\textsf{filter}(\mathcal{I}([t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]),\lambda x.\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),[x]}=\top)\\
\end{array}

For any tuple
t_{i}\in R
, let us discuss
\text{Del}(t_{i})
in two cases.

(a)

If
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\top
, then we know
\textsf{filter}([\mathcal{I}(t_{i})],\lambda x.\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),[x]}=\top)
is deleted because of the semantics of
filter
, and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime})=\top
because of
\text{Del}(t_{i}^{\prime})\leftrightarrow\text{Del}(t_{i})\lor\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]}
.

(b)

If
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\bot
, then we know the existence of
\textsf{filter}([\mathcal{I}(t_{i})],\lambda x.\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),[x]}=\top)
depends on
\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]}=\top
.
Let us discuss
\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]}
in two cases.

(b1)

If
\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]}=\top
is true, then
\textsf{filter}([\mathcal{I}(t_{i})],\lambda x.\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),[x]}=\top)
exists and

\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime})=\bot
because
\text{Del}(t_{i})\lor\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{i}]}=\top\to\text{Del}(t_{i}^{\prime})
.
Therefore,
\textsf{filter}([\mathcal{I}(t_{i})],

\lambda x.\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),[x]}

=\top)=[\mathcal{I}(t_{i}^{\prime})]
.

(b2)

If
\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]}=\top
is false, then
\textsf{filter}([\mathcal{I}(t_{i})],\lambda x.\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),[x]}=\top)
is deleted and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})

(t_{i}^{\prime})=\top
because
\text{Del}(t_{i})\lor\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{i}]}=\top\to\text{Del}(t_{i}^{\prime})
.
Therefore,
\textsf{filter}([\mathcal{I}(t_{i})],

\lambda x.\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),[x]}=\top)=[\mathcal{I}(t_{i}^{\prime})]
.

Thus,
\llbracket\sigma_{\phi}(Q)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}=\mathcal{I}(R)
by
3(a)
and
3(b)
and Theorem
4.6
for the inductive case
Q^{\prime}=\sigma_{\phi}(Q)
is proved.

(4)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime}=\rho_{T}(Q)
.

Suppose that
\Gamma\vdash Q\hookrightarrow R
where
R
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]
,
\Gamma\vdash\rho_{T}(Q)\hookrightarrow R^{\prime}
where
R^{\prime}
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,t_{n}^{\prime}]
by Figure
11
;
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\leadsto\Phi
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash\Phi\land\land_{i=1}^{n}t_{i}^{\prime}=t_{i}
by Figure
18
.

Take
\mathcal{I}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}\models\Phi\land\land_{i=1}^{n}t_{i}^{\prime}=t_{i}
.

\llbracket\rho_{T}(Q)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}=\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)[T\mapsto\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}]}=\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}=\mathcal{I}([t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}])=\mathcal{I}(R)
.

Thus, Theorem
4.6
for the inductive case
Q^{\prime}=\rho_{T}(Q)
is proved.

(5)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\times Q^{\prime}
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q:[a_{1},\ldots,a_{p}]
,
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q^{\prime}:[a_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,a_{q}^{\prime}]
by Figure
10
;
\Gamma\vdash Q\hookrightarrow R
where
R
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]
,
\Gamma\vdash Q^{\prime}\hookrightarrow R^{\prime}
where
R^{\prime}
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,t_{m}^{\prime}]
,
\Gamma\vdash Q\times Q^{\prime}\hookrightarrow R^{\prime\prime}
where
R^{\prime\prime}
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1,1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{n,m}^{\prime\prime}]
by Figure
11
;
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\leadsto\Phi
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q^{\prime}\leadsto\Phi^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\times Q^{\prime}\leadsto\Phi\land\Phi^{\prime}\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\land_{j=1}^{m}\Phi^{\prime\prime}_{i,j}
where
\Phi^{\prime\prime}_{i,j}=(\neg\text{Del}(t_{i})\land\neg\text{Del}(t_{j}^{\prime}))\to(\neg\text{Del}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})\land\land_{k=1}^{p}\llbracket a_{k}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime}]}=\llbracket a_{k}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{i}]}\land\land_{k=1}^{q}\llbracket a_{k}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime}]}=\llbracket a_{k}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{j}^{\prime}]})\land(\text{Del}(t_{i})\lor\text{Del}(t_{j}^{\prime})\to\text{Del}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime}))
by Figure
18
.

Take
\mathcal{I}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}\models\Phi\land\Phi^{\prime}\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\land_{j=1}^{m}\Phi^{\prime\prime}_{i,j}
.

By Figure
17
, we have

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\llbracket Q\times Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}&=&\textsf{foldl}(\lambda xs.\lambda x.\textsf{append}(xs,\textsf{map}(\llbracket Q_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)},\lambda y.\textsf{merge}(x,y))),[],\llbracket Q_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)})\\
&=&[\\
&&\quad[\textsf{merge}(\mathcal{I}(t_{1}),\mathcal{I}(t_{1}^{\prime})),\ldots,\textsf{merge}(\mathcal{I}(t_{1}),\mathcal{I}(t_{m}^{\prime}))]\\
&&\quad\ldots\\
&&\quad[\textsf{merge}(\mathcal{I}(t_{n}),\mathcal{I}(t_{1}^{\prime})),\ldots,\textsf{merge}(\mathcal{I}(t_{n}),\mathcal{I}(t_{m}^{\prime}))]\\
&&]\\
\end{array}

For any tuples
t_{i}\in R
and
t_{j}^{\prime}\in R^{\prime}
, let us discuss
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\lor\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})
in two cases.

(a)

If
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\lor\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})=\top
, then we know
\textsf{merge}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i}),\mathcal{I}(t_{j}^{\prime}))
is deleted by the semantics of
merge
and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})=\top
because of
\text{Del}(t_{i})\lor\text{Del}(t_{j}^{\prime})\to\text{Del}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})
.
Therefore,
\textsf{merge}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i}),\mathcal{I}(t_{j}^{\prime}))=\mathcal{I}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})
.

(b)

If
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})\lor\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})=\bot
, then we know
\textsf{merge}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i}),\mathcal{I}(t_{j}^{\prime}))=\mathcal{I}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})
by the semantics of
merge
and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})=\top
because of
\Phi_{i,j}^{\prime\prime}
.

Hence,
\llbracket Q\times Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}=\mathcal{I}(R)
by
5(a)
and
5(b)
and Theorem
4.6
for the inductive case
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\times Q^{\prime}
is proved.

(6)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\bowtie_{\phi}Q^{\prime}
.

This case can be proved by the inductive cases
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\times Q^{\prime}
and
Q^{\prime}=\sigma_{\phi}(Q)
.

(7)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q:[a_{1},\ldots,a_{p}]
,
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q^{\prime}:[a_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,a_{q}^{\prime}]
by Figure
10
;
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q\hookrightarrow R
where
R
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]
,
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q^{\prime}\hookrightarrow R^{\prime}
where
R^{\prime}
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,t_{m}^{\prime}]
,
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q\bowtie_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\hookrightarrow R
where
R^{\prime\prime}
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1,1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{n,m}^{\prime\prime},t_{1,m+1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{n,m+1}^{\prime\prime}]
by Figure
10
;
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\bowtie_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\leadsto\Phi
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\leadsto\Phi\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}^{\prime}
where
\Phi_{i}^{\prime}=\land_{k=1}^{p}\llbracket a_{k}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{i,m+1}^{\prime\prime}]}=\llbracket a_{k}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{i}]}\land\land_{k=1}^{q}\llbracket a_{k}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{i,m+1}^{\prime\prime}]}=\text{Null}\land(\neg\text{Del}(t_{i})\land\land_{j=1}^{m}\text{Del}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})\leftrightarrow\neg\text{Del}(t_{i,m+1}^{\prime\prime}))
by Figure
18
.

Take
\mathcal{I}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}\models\Phi\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}^{\prime}
.

By Figure
17
, we have

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\llbracket Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}&=&\textsf{foldl}(\lambda xs.\lambda x.\textsf{append}(xs,\textsf{ite}(|v_{1}(x)|=0,v_{2}(x),v_{1}(x)),[],\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}))\\
&=&\textsf{foldl}(\lambda xs.\lambda x.\textsf{append}(xs,\textsf{ite}(|v_{1}(x)|=0,v_{2}(x),v_{1}(x)),[],\mathcal{I}(\vec{t}_{1:n})))\\
&=&\textsf{foldl}(\lambda xs.\lambda x.\textsf{append}(xs,\textsf{ite}(|v_{1}(x)|=0,v_{2}(x),v_{1}(x)),\\
&&\quad[\textsf{ite}(|v_{1}(\mathcal{I}(t_{1}))|=0,v_{2}(\mathcal{I}(t_{1})),v_{1}(\mathcal{I}(t_{1})))],\mathcal{I}(\vec{t}_{2:n})))\\
&=&\ldots\\
&=&[\\
&&\quad\textsf{ite}(|v_{1}(\mathcal{I}(t_{1}))|=0,v_{2}(\mathcal{I}(t_{1})),v_{1}(\mathcal{I}(t_{1})))\\
&&\quad\ldots\\
&&\quad\textsf{ite}(|v_{1}(\mathcal{I}(t_{n}))|=0,v_{2}(\mathcal{I}(t_{n})),v_{1}(\mathcal{I}(t_{n})))\\
&&]\end{array}

For any entry
\textsf{ite}(|v_{1}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i}))|=0,v_{2}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i})),v_{1}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i})))\in\llbracket Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\rrbracket
, let us discuss
|v_{1}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i}))|=0
in two cases.

(a)

If
|v_{1}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i}))|=0
is true, then
|\llbracket[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]\bowtie_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}|=0
and
\textsf{ite}(|v_{1}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i}))|=0,v_{2}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i})),\\

v_{1}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i})))=v_{2}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i}))=[\textsf{merge}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i}),T_{\text{Null}})]
.

Further, if
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\top
, then
\textsf{ite}(|v_{1}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i}))|=0,v_{2}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i})),v_{1}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i})))=\textsf{merge}

(\mathcal{I}(t_{i}),\\

T_{\text{Null}})
is deleted by the semantics of
merge
. Meanwhile,
\mathcal{I}([t_{i,1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{i,m}^{\prime\prime},t_{i,m+1}^{\prime\prime}])
are all deleted because of
\text{Del}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})\leftrightarrow\text{Del}(t_{i})\lor\text{Del}(t_{j}^{\prime})\lor\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),[\mathcal{I}(\textsf{merge}(t_{i},t_{j}^{\prime}))]}\neq\top
for
1\leq j\leq m
and
\text{Del}(t_{i,m+1}^{\prime\prime})\leftrightarrow\text{Del}(t_{i})\lor\neg\land_{j=1}^{m}\text{Del}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})
.
Therefore,
\textsf{ite}(|v_{1}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i}))|=0,v_{2}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i})),\\

v_{1}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i})))=\mathcal{I}

([t_{i,1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{i,m}^{\prime\prime},t_{i,m+1}^{\prime\prime}])
.

Otherwise, if
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\bot
, then
\textsf{merge}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i}),T_{\text{Null}})=\mathcal{I}(t_{i,m+1}^{\prime\prime})
.
Also, since
\sigma_{\phi}(\mathcal{I}(

[\textsf{merge}(t_{i},t_{1}^{\prime}),\ldots,\textsf{merge}(t_{i},t_{m}^{\prime})]))
and
\mathcal{I}([t_{i,1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{i,m}^{\prime\prime}])
are deleted,
\textsf{ite}(|v_{1}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i}))|=0,\\

v_{2}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i})),v_{1}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i})))=[\textsf{merge}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i}),T_{\text{Null}})]=[\mathcal{I}(t_{i,m+1}^{\prime\prime})]
.

(b)

If
|v_{1}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i}))|=0
is false, then
|\llbracket[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]\bowtie_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}|\neq 0
and
\textsf{ite}(|v_{1}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i}))|=0,v_{2}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i})),\\

v_{1}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i})))=v_{1}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i}))=\llbracket[\mathcal{I}(t_{i})]\bowtie_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}=\sigma_{\phi}(\mathcal{I}([\textsf{merge}(t_{i},t_{j}^{\prime}),

\ldots,\textsf{merge}(t_{i},t_{j}^{\prime})]))
.

Further, if
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\top
, then
\mathcal{I}(\sigma_{\phi}([\textsf{merge}(t_{i},t_{j}^{\prime}),\ldots,\textsf{merge}(t_{i},t_{j}^{\prime})]))
are deleted by the semantics of
merge
.
Meanwhile,
\mathcal{I}([t_{i,1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{i,m}^{\prime\prime},t_{i,m+1}^{\prime\prime}])
are all deleted because of
\text{Del}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})\leftrightarrow\text{Del}(t_{i})\lor\text{Del}(t_{j}^{\prime})\lor\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),[\mathcal{I}(\textsf{merge}(t_{i},t_{j}^{\prime}))]}\neq\top
for
1\leq j\leq m
and
\text{Del}(t_{i,m+1}^{\prime\prime})\leftrightarrow\text{Del}(t_{i})\lor\neg\land_{j=1}^{m}\text{Del}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})
.
Therefore,
\textsf{ite}(|v_{1}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i}))|=0,v_{2}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i})),

v_{1}(\mathcal{I}(t_{i})))=\\

\mathcal{I}

([t_{i,1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{i,m}^{\prime\prime},t_{i,m+1}^{\prime\prime}])
.

Otherwise, if
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\bot
,
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{k}^{\prime})=\bot
and
\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),[\mathcal{I}(\textsf{merge}(t_{i},t_{k}^{\prime}))]}=\top
where
1\leq k\leq m
, then
\mathcal{I}(t_{i,k}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\textsf{merge}(t_{i},t_{k}^{\prime}))
and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i,k}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(\textsf{merge}(t_{i},t_{k}^{\prime}))=\bot
.

Hence,
\llbracket Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}=\mathcal{I}(R)
by
7(a)
and
7(b)
and Theorem
4.6
for the inductive case
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}
is proved.

(8)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\mathbin{\bowtie\mkern-5.8mu\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}
.

Following a similar method in the inductive case
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}
, we prove
\llbracket Q\mathbin{\bowtie\mkern-5.8mu\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}=\mathcal{I}(R)
.

(9)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie\mkern-5.8mu\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}
.

Suppose that by
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q:[a_{1},\ldots,a_{p}]
,
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q:[a_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,a_{q}^{\prime}]
by Figure
10
;
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q\hookrightarrow R
where
R
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]
,
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q^{\prime}\hookrightarrow R^{\prime}
where
R^{\prime}
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,t_{m}^{\prime}]
,
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie\mkern-5.8mu\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\hookrightarrow R^{\prime\prime}
where
R^{\prime\prime}
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1,1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{n+1,m}^{\prime\prime},t_{1,m+1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{n,m+1}^{\prime\prime}]
, by Figure
11
;
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\leadsto\Phi
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie\mkern-5.8mu\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\leadsto\Phi\land\land_{j=1}^{m}\Phi_{j}^{\prime}
where
\Phi_{j}^{\prime}=\land_{k=1}^{p}\llbracket a_{k}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{n+1,j}^{\prime\prime}]}=\text{Null}\land\land_{k=1}^{q}\llbracket a_{k}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{n+1,j}^{\prime\prime}]}=\llbracket a_{k}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{j}^{\prime}]}\land(\neg\text{Del}(t_{j}^{\prime})\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\text{Del}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})\leftrightarrow\neg\text{Del}(t_{n+1,j}^{\prime\prime}))
by Figure
18
.

Take
\mathcal{I}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}\models\Phi\land\land_{j=1}^{m}\Phi_{j}^{\prime}
.

By the inductive hypothesis, we know
\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}=\mathcal{I}(R)
,
\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}=\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime})
and
\llbracket Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}=\mathcal{I}([t_{1,1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{n,m+1}^{\prime\prime}])
.

For any tuple
t_{j}^{\prime}\in\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}
, if
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})=\top
, then
|\llbracket Q\bowtie_{\phi}[t_{j}^{\prime}]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}|=0
is true and, therefore,
\textsf{filter}([\mathcal{I}(t_{j}^{\prime})],\lambda y.|\llbracket Q\bowtie_{\phi}[y]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}|=0)
is deleted because of the semantics of
filter
and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{n+1,j}^{\prime\prime})=\top
because of
\neg\text{Del}(t_{j}^{\prime})\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\text{Del}(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})\leftrightarrow\neg\text{Del}(t_{n+1,j}^{\prime\prime})
.
Otherwise,
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})=\bot
and then let us discuss
|\llbracket Q\bowtie_{\phi}[t_{j}^{\prime}]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}|=0
in two cases.

(a)

If
|\llbracket Q\bowtie_{\phi}[t_{j}^{\prime}]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}|=0
is true, then
\textsf{map}(\textsf{filter}([\mathcal{I}(t_{j}^{\prime})],\lambda y.|\llbracket Q\bowtie_{\phi}[y]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}|=0),\\

\lambda x.\textsf{merge}(T_{\text{Null}},x))=[\textsf{merge}

(T_{\text{Null}},\mathcal{I}(t_{j}^{\prime}))]
,
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i,j}^{\prime\prime})=\top
for
1\leq i\leq n
,
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{n+1,j}^{\prime\prime})\\

=\bot
and
\mathcal{I}(t_{n+1,j}^{\prime\prime})=\textsf{merge}(T_{\text{Null}},\mathcal{I}(t_{j}^{\prime}))
because of
\Phi_{j}^{\prime}
and the semantics of
merge
.

(b)

If
|\llbracket Q\bowtie_{\phi}[t_{i}]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}|=0
is false, then
\textsf{map}(\textsf{filter}([\mathcal{I}(t_{i})],\lambda y.|\llbracket Q\bowtie_{\phi}[y]\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}|=0),\lambda x.

\textsf{merge}(T_{\text{Null}},x))
is deleted and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{n+1,j}^{\prime})=\top
.

Hence,
\llbracket Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie\mkern-5.8mu\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}=\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime\prime})
by
10(a)
and
10(b)
and Theorem
4.6
for the inductive case
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\mathbin{\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}\mkern-5.8mu\bowtie\mkern-5.8mu\rule[0.73193pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\rule[3.7889pt]{2.5pt}{0.6pt}}}_{\phi}Q^{\prime}
is proved.

(10)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime}=\text{Distinct}(Q)
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\hookrightarrow R
where
R
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash\text{Distinct}(Q)\hookrightarrow R^{\prime}
where
R^{\prime}
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,t_{n}^{\prime}]
by Figure
11
;
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\leadsto\Phi
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\leadsto\Phi\land\Phi^{\prime}
where
\Phi^{\prime}=\text{Dedup}(\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime}})
by Figure
18
.

By the inductive hypothesis, we have
\mathcal{I},\mathcal{D}\models\Phi
and
\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}=\mathcal{I}(R)
.

Take
\mathcal{I}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}\models\Phi\land\Phi^{\prime}
.

For any tuple
t_{i}\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}
, if
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\top
, then
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime})=\top
.
On the other hand, for any existing tuples
t_{i}
and
t_{j}
in
\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\bot
and
i\leq j
, let us discuss
\mathcal{I}(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(t_{j})
in two cases.

(a)

If
\mathcal{I}(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(t_{j})
is true, then
\mathcal{I}(t_{i}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(t_{i})
and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\bot
, and
\textsf{filter}(\mathcal{I}(t_{j}),\lambda y.\mathcal{I}(t_{i})=y)
is deleted and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})=\top
by the semantics of
filter
.

(b)

If
\mathcal{I}(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(t_{j})
is false, then
\mathcal{I}(t_{i}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(t_{i})
and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\bot
, and
\mathcal{I}(t_{j}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(t_{j})
and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j}^{\prime})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{j})=\bot
by the semantics of
filter
.

Hence,
\llbracket\text{Distinct}(Q)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}=\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime\prime})
by
10(a)
and
10(b)
and Theorem
4.6
for the inductive case
Q^{\prime}=\text{Distinct}(Q)
is proved.

(11)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\cap Q^{\prime}
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{S}\vdash\text{Distinct}(Q)\hookrightarrow R
where
R
ash symbolic tuples
[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]
,
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q^{\prime}\hookrightarrow R^{\prime}
where
R^{\prime}
ash symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,t_{m}^{\prime}]
,
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q\cap Q^{\prime}\hookrightarrow R^{\prime\prime}
where
R^{\prime\prime}
ash symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{n}^{\prime\prime}]
by Figure
11
;
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash\text{Distinct}(Q)\leadsto\Phi
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q^{\prime}\leadsto\Phi^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\cap Q^{\prime}\leadsto\Phi\land\Phi^{\prime}\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}^{\prime\prime}
where
\Phi_{i}^{\prime\prime}=t_{i}\in\vec{t^{\prime}}\to t_{i}^{\prime\prime}=t_{i}\land t_{i}\not\in\vec{t^{\prime}}\to\text{Del}(t_{i}^{\prime\prime})
by Figure
18
.

By the inductive hypothesis, we know
\llbracket\text{Distinct}(Q)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}=\mathcal{I}(R)
and
\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}=\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime})
.

Take
\mathcal{I}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}\models\Phi\land\Phi^{\prime}\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\land_{j=1}^{m}\Phi_{i,j}^{\prime\prime}
.

For any tuple
\mathcal{I}(t_{i})\in\llbracket\text{Distinct}(Q)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}
, if
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\top
, then
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime})=\top
because of
t_{i}\not\in\vec{t^{\prime}}\to\text{Del}(t_{i}^{\prime\prime})
; otherwise, let us discuss
\mathcal{I}(t_{i})\in\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}
in two cases.

(a)

If
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\bot
and
\mathcal{I}(t_{i})\in\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}
is true, then
\mathcal{I}(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(t_{i}^{\prime})
and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime})=\bot
by the semantics of
filter
.

(b)

If
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\bot
and
\mathcal{I}(t_{i})\in\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}
is false, then
\textsf{filter}([t_{i}],\lambda x.x\in\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)})
is deleted by the semantics of
filter
and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime})=\top
because of
t_{i}\not\in\vec{t^{\prime}}\to\text{Del}(t_{i}^{\prime\prime})
.

Hence,
\llbracket Q\cap Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}=\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime\prime})
by
11(a)
and
11(b)
and Theorem
4.6
for the inductive case
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\cap Q^{\prime}
is proved.

(12)

Inductive case:
Q=Q_{1}\setminus Q_{2}
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{S}\vdash\text{Distinct}(Q)\hookrightarrow R
where
R
ash symbolic tuples
[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]
,
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q^{\prime}\hookrightarrow R^{\prime}
where
R^{\prime}
ash symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,t_{m}^{\prime}]
,
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q\setminus Q^{\prime}\hookrightarrow R^{\prime\prime}
where
R^{\prime\prime}
ash symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{n}^{\prime\prime}]
by Figure
11
;
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash\text{Distinct}(Q)\leadsto\Phi
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q^{\prime}\leadsto\Phi^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\setminus Q^{\prime}\leadsto\Phi\land\Phi^{\prime}\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}^{\prime\prime}
where
\Phi_{i}^{\prime\prime}=t_{i}\not\in\vec{t^{\prime}}\to t_{i}^{\prime\prime}=t_{i}\land t_{i}\in\vec{t^{\prime}}\to\text{Del}(t_{i}^{\prime\prime})
by Figure
18
.

By the inductive hypothesis, we know
\llbracket\text{Distinct}(Q)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}=\mathcal{I}(R)
and
\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}=\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime})
.

Take
\mathcal{I}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}\models\Phi\land\Phi^{\prime}\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\land_{j=1}^{m}\Phi_{i,j}^{\prime\prime}
.

For any tuple
\mathcal{I}(t_{i})\in\llbracket\text{Distinct}(Q)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}
, if
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\top
, then
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime})=\top
because of
t_{i}\in\vec{t^{\prime}}\to t_{i}^{\prime\prime}=t_{i}
; otherwise, let us discuss
\mathcal{I}(t_{i})\in\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}
in two cases.

(a)

If
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\bot
and
\mathcal{I}(t_{i})\in\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}
is true, then
\textsf{filter}([t_{i}],\lambda x.x\not\in\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)})
is deleted by the semantics of
filter
and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime})=\top
because of
t_{i}\in\vec{t^{\prime}}\to\text{Del}(t_{i}^{\prime\prime})
.

(b)

If
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\bot
and
\mathcal{I}(t_{i})\in\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}
is false, then
\mathcal{I}(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(t_{i}^{\prime})
and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime})=\bot
by the semantics of
filter
.

Hence,
\llbracket Q\setminus Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}=\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime\prime})
by
12(a)
and
12(b)
and Theorem
4.6
for the inductive case
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\setminus Q^{\prime}
is proved.

(13)

Inductive case:
Q=Q_{1}-Q_{2}
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q\hookrightarrow R
where
R
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]
,
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q^{\prime}\hookrightarrow R^{\prime}
where
R^{\prime}
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,t_{m}^{\prime}]
,
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q-Q^{\prime}\hookrightarrow R^{\prime\prime}
where
R^{\prime\prime}
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{n}^{\prime\prime}]
by Figure
11
;
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\leadsto\Phi
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q^{\prime}\leadsto\Phi^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q-Q^{\prime}\leadsto\Phi\land\Phi^{\prime}\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\land_{j=1}^{m}\Phi_{i,j}^{\prime\prime}
where
\Phi_{i,j}^{\prime\prime}=\neg\text{Del}(t_{i})\land\neg\text{Paired}(i,j)\to t_{i}^{\prime\prime}=t_{i}\land\text{Del}(t_{i})\lor\text{Paired}(i,j)\to\text{Del}(t_{i}^{\prime\prime})
by Figure
19
.

By the inductive hypothesis, we know
\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}=\mathcal{I}(R)
and
\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}=\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime})
.

Take
\mathcal{I}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}\models\Phi\land\Phi^{\prime}\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\land_{j=1}^{m}\Phi_{i,j}^{\prime\prime}
.

(14)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\cup Q^{\prime}
.

Since
Q\cup Q^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow\text{Distinct}(Q\mathrel{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\uplus$}}}Q^{\prime})
, this case can be proved by the inductive cases
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\mathrel{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\uplus$}}}Q^{\prime}
and
Q^{\prime}=\text{Distinct}(Q\mathrel{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\uplus$}}}Q^{\prime})
.

(15)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\uplus Q^{\prime}
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q\hookrightarrow R
where
R
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]
,
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q^{\prime}\hookrightarrow R^{\prime}
where
R^{\prime}
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,t_{m}^{\prime}]
,
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q\uplus Q^{\prime}\hookrightarrow R^{\prime\prime}
where
R^{\prime\prime}
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{n+m}^{\prime\prime}]
by Figure
11
;
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\leadsto\Phi
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q^{\prime}\leadsto\Phi^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\uplus Q^{\prime}\leadsto\Phi\land\Phi^{\prime}\land\Phi^{\prime\prime}
where
\Phi^{\prime\prime}=\land_{i=1}^{n}t_{i}^{\prime\prime}=t_{i}\land\land_{j=n+1}^{n+m}t_{j}^{\prime\prime}=t_{j-n}^{\prime}
by Figure
18
.

By the inductive hypothesis, we know
\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}=\mathcal{I}(R)
and
\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}=\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime})
.

Take
\mathcal{I}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}\models\Phi\land\Phi^{\prime}\land\Phi^{\prime\prime}
.

Further, since
t_{i}^{\prime\prime}=t_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq n
and
t_{j}^{\prime\prime}=t_{j}
for
n+1\leq j\leq n+m
, we have

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\llbracket Q\uplus Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}&=&\textsf{append}(\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)},\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)})\\
&=&\textsf{append}(\mathcal{I}(R),\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime}))\\
&=&\textsf{append}([\mathcal{I}(t_{1}),\ldots,\mathcal{I}(t_{n})],[\mathcal{I}(t_{1}^{\prime}),\ldots,\mathcal{I}(t_{m}^{\prime})])\\
&=&[\mathcal{I}(t_{1}),\ldots,\mathcal{I}(t_{n}),\mathcal{I}(t_{1}^{\prime}),\ldots,\mathcal{I}(t_{m}^{\prime})]\\
&=&\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime\prime})\\
\end{array}

Hence, Theorem
4.6
for the inductive case
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\uplus Q^{\prime}
is proved.

(16)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q\mathrel{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\uplus$}}}Q^{\prime}
.

Since
Q\mathrel{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{180.0}{$\uplus$}}}Q^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow Q-(Q-Q^{\prime})
, this case can be proved by the inductive case
Q^{\prime\prime}=Q-Q^{\prime}
.

(17)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime}=\text{GroupBy}(Q,\vec{E},L,\phi)
.

Suppose that
\Gamma\vdash Q\hookrightarrow R
where
R
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]
,
\Gamma\vdash\text{GroupBy}(Q,\vec{E},L,\phi)\hookrightarrow R
where
R
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,t_{n}^{\prime}]
by Figure
11
;
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\leadsto\Phi
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash\text{GroupBy}(Q,\vec{E},

L,\phi)\leadsto\Phi\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}^{\prime}\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}^{\prime\prime}
where
\Phi_{i}^{\prime}=\Sigma_{j=1}^{n}\text{If}(g(t_{i},j),0,1)=\text{If}(\text{Del}(t_{i}),0,1)\land\land_{j=1}^{i-1}g(t_{i},j)=(\neg\text{Del}(t_{i})\land g(t_{i},j)\land\llbracket\vec{E}\rrbracket)_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{i}]}=\llbracket\vec{E}\rrbracket)_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{i}j}
and
\Phi_{i}^{\prime\prime}=(g(t_{i},j)\land\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,g^{-1}(i)}=\top\to\neg\text{Del}(t_{i}^{\prime})\land\land_{k=1}^{l}\llbracket a_{k}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t_{i}^{\prime}]}=\llbracket a_{k}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,g^{-1}(i)})\land(\neg g(t_{i},j)\lor\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,g^{-1}(i)}\neq\top\to\text{Del}(t_{i}^{\prime}))
by Figure
18
;
L=[a_{1},\ldots a_{l}]
.

By the inductive hypothesis, we have
\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}=\mathcal{I}(R)
.

Take
\mathcal{I}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}\models\Phi\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}^{\prime}\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}^{\prime\prime}
.

Let
G_{i}
be the
i
-th group of
\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}
and
g^{-1}(i)
be the function takes the index of the group
G_{i}
and returns the tuples of the group
G_{i}
.
Assume there are
m
non-empty groups in
\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}
s.t.
m\leq n
and the other
n-m
empty groups are deleted because of no non-deleted tuple.
Therefore, we know that for each group
G_{i}
, (i) it must have at least one non-deleted tuple, (ii) all tuples
g^{-1}(i)
in
G_{i}
must share the same values over
\vec{E}
and (iii)
\llbracket\vec{E}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(g^{-1}(i))}
is unique among those groups because of Figure
18
.

Further, by the semantics of
filter
and
\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}=\mathcal{I}(R)
, we know
\text{Dedup}(Q,\vec{E})
denotes
m
unique values over
\vec{E}
.
Therefore,
Gs
in
\llbracket\text{GroupBy}(Q,\vec{E},L,\phi)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}
is equivalent to the groups
\mathcal{I}([G_{1},\ldots,G_{m}])
in the formula
\Phi_{i}^{\prime\prime}
and
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\top
since
G_{i}
is empty group for
m<i\leq n
.

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\llbracket\text{GroupBy}(Q,\vec{E},L,\phi)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}&=&\textsf{map}(\textsf{filter}(Gs,\lambda xs.\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),xs}=\top),\lambda xs.\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),xs})\\
&=&\textsf{map}(\textsf{filter}([G_{1},\ldots,G_{m}],\lambda xs.\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),xs}=\top),\lambda xs.\llbracket L\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),xs})\\
&=&[\mathcal{I}(t_{1}^{\prime}),\ldots,\mathcal{I}(t_{m}^{\prime})]\\
&=&\textsf{append}([\mathcal{I}(t_{1}^{\prime}),\ldots,\mathcal{I}(t_{m}^{\prime})],[\mathcal{I}(t_{m+1}),\ldots,\mathcal{I}(t_{n})])\\
&=&[\mathcal{I}(t_{1}^{\prime}),\ldots,\mathcal{I}(t_{m}^{\prime}),\mathcal{I}(t_{m+1}^{\prime}),\ldots,\mathcal{I}(t_{n}^{\prime})]\\
&=&\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime})\end{array}

Hence, Theorem
4.6
for the inductive case
Q^{\prime}=\text{GroupBy}(Q,\vec{E},L,\phi)
is proved.

(18)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime\prime}=\text{With}(\vec{Q},\vec{T},Q^{\prime})
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{S}\vdash Q_{i}:\mathcal{A}_{i}
by Figure
10
;
\Gamma\vdash Q_{i}\hookrightarrow R_{i}
by Figure
11
;
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q_{i}\leadsto\Phi_{i}
by Figure
18
.

By the inductive hypothesis, we have
\mathcal{I}(R_{i})=\llbracket Q_{i}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}
where
1\leq i\leq n
and
n=|\vec{Q}|=|\vec{T}|
;
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash Q^{\prime}\hookrightarrow R^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{S}^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash Q^{\prime}\leadsto\Phi^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime})=\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma^{\prime})}
where
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}=\mathcal{S}[T_{1}\mapsto\mathcal{A}_{1},\ldots,T_{n}\mapsto\mathcal{A}_{n}]
and
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma[T_{1}\mapsto\mathcal{T}_{1},\ldots,T_{n}\mapsto\mathcal{T}_{n}]
.

Take
\mathcal{I}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}\models\land_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}\land\Phi^{\prime}
.

By Figure
17
, we know
\llbracket\text{With}(\vec{Q},\vec{T},Q^{\prime})\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma^{\prime})}=\llbracket Q^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma^{\prime})}=\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime})
.

Hence, Theorem
4.6
for the inductive case
Q^{\prime\prime}=\text{With}(\vec{Q},\vec{T},Q^{\prime})
is proved.

(19)

Inductive case:
Q^{\prime}=\text{OrderBy}(Q,\vec{E},b)
.

Suppose that
\Gamma\vdash Q\hookrightarrow R
where
R
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}]
,
\Gamma\vdash\text{OrderBy}(Q,\vec{E},b)\hookrightarrow R^{\prime}
where
R^{\prime}
has symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,t_{n}^{\prime}]
by Figure
11
;

\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash Q\leadsto\Phi
,
\mathcal{S},\Gamma\vdash

\text{OrderBy}(Q,\vec{E},b)\leadsto\Phi\land\Phi^{\prime}\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}^{\prime\prime}
where
\Phi^{\prime}=\text{moveDelToEnd}(\vec{t},\vec{t^{\prime\prime}})
and
\Phi_{i}^{\prime\prime}=t_{i}^{\prime}=t_{\text{find}(i,\vec{E},b)}^{\prime\prime}
by Figure
19
;
R^{\prime\prime}
is the fresh symbolic table where
R^{\prime\prime}
has the symbolic tuples
[t_{1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{n}^{\prime\prime}]
and
t_{\text{indexOf}(\vec{t},i)}=t_{i}^{\prime\prime}
for any tuple
t_{i}^{\prime\prime}\in R^{\prime\prime}
.

By the inductive hypothesis, we have
\mathcal{I}(R)=\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}
. Also, by the semantics of the
moveDelToEnd
function,
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{\text{indexOf}(\vec{t},i)})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i}^{\prime\prime})
and
\mathcal{I}(t_{\text{indexOf}(\vec{t},i)})=\mathcal{I}(t_{i}^{\prime\prime})
.

Take
\mathcal{I}
s.t.
\mathcal{I}\models\Phi\land\Phi^{\prime}\land\land_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{i}^{\prime\prime}
.

Assume there are
m
non-deleted symbolic tuples in
R
where
m\leq n
, then we have, for any tuple
t_{i}^{\prime\prime}\in[t_{1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{m}^{\prime\prime}]
,
\mathcal{I}(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(t_{\text{indexOf}(\vec{t},i)}^{\prime\prime})
and
\mathcal{I}(del)(t_{i})=\mathcal{I}(del)(t_{\text{indexOf}(\vec{t},i)}^{\prime\prime})=\bot
and the other tuples
[t_{m+1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{n}^{\prime\prime}]
are deleted (i.e.,
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t_{i})=\top
where
m<i\leq n
) by the semantics of the
moveDelToEnd
function.
Let
a_{i}
denote the
i
-th smallest (biggest) tuple from
\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}-xs
(i.e.,
a_{i}=\text{MinTuple}(\vec{E},b,

\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}-xs)
where
xs=[a_{i},\ldots,a_{i-1}]
), then

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\llbracket\text{OrderBy}(Q,\vec{E},b)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}&=&\textsf{foldl}(\lambda xs.\lambda\_.(\textsf{append}(xs,[\text{MinTuple}(\vec{E},b,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}-xs)])),\\
&&\quad[],\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)})\\
&=&\textsf{foldl}(\lambda xs.\lambda\_.(\textsf{append}(xs,[\text{MinTuple}(\vec{E},b,\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}-xs)])),\\
&&\quad[],\mathcal{I}(R))\\
&&\ldots\\
&=&[\mathcal{I}(a_{1}),\ldots,\mathcal{I}(a_{m})]\\
&=&\mathcal{I}([a_{1},\ldots,a_{m}])\\
\end{array}

Furthermore, let
t_{\text{indexOf}(\vec{t},i)}
be the corresponding tuple of
a_{i}\in\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}
,

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\llbracket\text{OrderBy}$ $(Q,\vec{E},b)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}&=&\mathcal{I}([a_{1},\ldots,a_{m}])\\
&=&\mathcal{I}([t_{\text{indexOf}(\vec{t},i)},\ldots,t_{\text{indexOf}(\vec{t},i)}])\\
&=&\mathcal{I}([t_{1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{m}^{\prime\prime}])\\
&=&\mathcal{I}([t_{1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{m}^{\prime\prime}])+\mathcal{I}([t_{m+1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{n}^{\prime\prime}])\\
&=&\mathcal{I}([t_{1}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{n}^{\prime\prime}])\\
&=&\mathcal{I}([t_{\text{find}(1,\vec{E},b)}^{\prime\prime},\ldots,t_{\text{find}(n,\vec{E},b)}^{\prime\prime}])\\
&=&\mathcal{I}([t_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,t_{n}^{\prime}])\\
&=&\mathcal{I}(R^{\prime})\\
\end{array}

Hence, Theorem
4.6
for the inductive case
Q^{\prime}=\text{OrderBy}(Q,\vec{E},b)
is proved.

∎

Lemma D.1.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a database over schema
\mathcal{S}
and
xs
be a tuple list which formulates a predicate
\phi
.
Consider a symbolic database
\Gamma
over
\mathcal{S}
and a list of symbolic tuples
\mathcal{T}
such that
\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}
is valid.
For any interpretation
\mathcal{I}
such that
\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)=\mathcal{D}\land\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})=xs
, evaluating
\phi
over the concrete database
\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)
and the concrete tuple list
\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})
yields the value of
\mathcal{I}(\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})
, i.e.,

\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)=\mathcal{D}\land\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})=xs\Rightarrow\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\mathcal{I}(\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})

Lemma D.2.

Suppose
\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},xs}
is valid, then
\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)=\mathcal{D}\land\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})=xs\Rightarrow\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\mathcal{I}(\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})
holds.

Proof.

By structural induction on
\phi
.

(1)

Base case:
\phi=\top
.

\llbracket\top\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}=\top
by Figure
21
. Also,
\llbracket\top\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\top
by Figure
17
. Therefore,
\mathcal{I}(\llbracket\top\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})=\mathcal{I}(\top)=\top=\llbracket\top\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}
.

(2)

Base case:
\phi=\bot
.

\llbracket\bot\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}=\bot
by Figure
21
. Also,
\llbracket\bot\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\bot
by Figure
17
. Therefore,
\mathcal{I}(\llbracket\bot\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})=\mathcal{I}(\bot)=\bot=\llbracket\bot\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}
.

(3)

Base case:
\phi=\text{Null}
.

\llbracket\text{Null}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}=\text{Null}
by Figure
21
. Also,
\llbracket\text{Null}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\text{Null}
by Figure
17
. Therefore,
\mathcal{I}(\llbracket\text{Null}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})=\mathcal{I}(\text{Null})=\text{Null}=\llbracket\text{Null}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}
.

(4)

Base case:
\phi=A_{1}\odot A_{2}
.

\llbracket A_{1}\odot A_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}=\textsf{ite}(v_{1}=\text{Null}\lor v_{2}=\text{Null},\bot,v_{1}\odot v_{2})
where
v_{1}=\llbracket A_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}
and
v_{2}=\llbracket A_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}
by Figure
21
.

\llbracket A_{1}\odot A_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\textsf{ite}(v_{1}^{\prime}=\text{Null}\lor v_{2}^{\prime}=\text{Null},\bot,v_{1}^{\prime}\odot v_{2}^{\prime})
where
v_{1}^{\prime}=\llbracket A_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}
and
v_{2}^{\prime}=\llbracket A_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}
by Figure
17
.
By Lemma
D.3
, we have
\llbracket A_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\mathcal{I}(\llbracket A_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})
and
\llbracket A_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\mathcal{I}(\llbracket A_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})
.
Also since
\mathcal{I}(v_{1})=\mathcal{I}(\llbracket A_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})=\\

\llbracket A_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}

=v_{1}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{I}(v_{2})=v_{2}^{\prime}
,

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathcal{I}(\llbracket A_{1}\odot A_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})&=&\mathcal{I}(\textsf{ite}(v_{1}=\text{Null}\lor v_{2}=\text{Null},\bot,v_{1}\odot v_{2}))\\
&=&\textsf{ite}(\mathcal{I}(v_{1})=\text{Null}\lor\mathcal{I}(v_{2})=\text{Null},\bot,\mathcal{I}(v_{1})\odot\mathcal{I}(v_{2}))\\
&=&\textsf{ite}(v_{1}^{\prime}=\text{Null}\lor v_{2}^{\prime}=\text{Null},\bot,v_{1}^{\prime}\odot v_{2}^{\prime})\\
&=&\llbracket A_{1}\odot A_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}\\
\end{array}

(5)

Base case:
\phi=\text{IsNull}(E)
.

\llbracket\text{IsNull}(E)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}=\textsf{ite}(\llbracket E\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}=\text{Null},\top,\bot)
by Figure
21
.

\llbracket\text{IsNull}(E)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\textsf{ite}(

\llbracket E\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\text{Null},\top,\bot)
by Figure
17
.
By Lemma
D.3
, we have
\llbracket E\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\mathcal{I}(\llbracket E\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})
.

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathcal{I}(\llbracket\text{IsNull}(E)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})&=&\mathcal{I}(\textsf{ite}(\llbracket E\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}=\text{Null},\top,\bot))\\
&=&\textsf{ite}(\mathcal{I}(\llbracket E\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})=\text{Null},\top,\bot)\\
&=&\textsf{ite}(\llbracket E\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\text{Null},\top,\bot)\\
&=&\llbracket\text{IsNull}(E)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}\end{array}

(6)

Base case:
\phi=\vec{E}\in\vec{v}
.

\llbracket\vec{E}\in\vec{v}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}=\llbracket\lor_{i=1}^{n}\land_{j=1}^{m}E_{j}=v_{i,j}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}
where
\vec{v}=[v_{1,1},\ldots,v_{n,m}]
and
m=|\vec{E}|
by Figure
21
.

\llbracket\vec{E}\in\vec{v}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\lor_{i=1}^{n}\llbracket\vec{E}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\llbracket v_{i}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}
by Figure
17
.
By Lemma
D.3
, we have
\llbracket E_{j}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\mathcal{I}(\llbracket E_{j}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})
and
\llbracket v_{i,j}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\mathcal{I}(\llbracket v_{i,j}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})
.

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathcal{I}(\llbracket\vec{E}\in\vec{v}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})&=&\mathcal{I}(\llbracket\lor_{i=1}^{n}\land_{j=1}^{m}E_{j}=v_{i,j}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})\\
&=&\lor_{i=1}^{n}\land_{j=1}^{m}\mathcal{I}(\llbracket E_{j}=v_{i,j}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})\\
&=&\lor_{i=1}^{n}\land_{j=1}^{m}\mathcal{I}(\llbracket E_{j}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})=\mathcal{I}(\llbracket v_{i,j}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})\\
&=&\lor_{i=1}^{n}\land_{j=1}^{m}\llbracket E_{j}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\llbracket v_{i,j}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}\\
&=&\llbracket\vec{E}\in\vec{v}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}\\
\end{array}

(7)

Base case:
\phi=\vec{E}\in Q
.

Suppose that
\llbracket Q\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)}=\vec{v}=[v_{1,1},\ldots,v_{n,m}]
by .

(8)

Inductive case:
\phi=\phi_{1}\land\phi_{2}
.

\llbracket\phi_{1}\land\phi_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}=\text{ite}((v_{1}=v_{2}=\text{Null})\land(v_{1}=\text{Null}\land v_{2}=\bot)\land(v_{1}=\bot\land v_{2}=\text{Null}),\text{Null},v_{1}=\top\land v_{2}=\top)
where
v_{1}=\llbracket\phi_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}
and
v_{2}=\llbracket\phi_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}
by Figure
21
.

\llbracket\phi_{1}\land\phi_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\text{ite}((v_{1}^{\prime}=v_{2}^{\prime}=\text{Null})\land(v_{1}^{\prime}=\text{Null}\land v_{2}^{\prime}=\bot)\land(v_{1}^{\prime}=\bot\land v_{2}^{\prime}=\text{Null}),\text{Null},v_{1}^{\prime}=\top\land v_{2}^{\prime}=\top)
where
v_{1}^{\prime}=\llbracket\phi_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}
and
v_{2}^{\prime}=\llbracket\phi_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}
by Figure
17
.
By Lemma
D.1
, we have
\llbracket\phi_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\mathcal{I}(\llbracket\phi_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})
and
\llbracket\phi_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\mathcal{I}(\llbracket\phi_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})
.
Also since
\mathcal{I}(v_{1})=\mathcal{I}(\llbracket\phi_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})=\llbracket\phi_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=v_{1}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{I}(v_{2})=v_{2}^{\prime}
,

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathcal{I}(\llbracket\phi_{1}\land\phi_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})&=&\mathcal{I}(\text{ite}((v_{1}=\text{Null}\land v_{2}=\text{Null})\land(v_{1}=\text{Null}\land v_{2}=\bot)\\
&&\qquad\land(v_{1}=\bot\land v_{2}=\text{Null}),\text{Null},v_{1}=\top\land v_{2}=\top))\\
&=&\text{ite}((\mathcal{I}(v_{1})=\text{Null}\land\mathcal{I}(v_{2})=\text{Null})\land(\mathcal{I}(v_{1})=\text{Null}\land\mathcal{I}(v_{2})=\bot)\\
&&\qquad\land(\mathcal{I}(v_{1})=\bot\land\mathcal{I}(v_{2})=\text{Null}),\text{Null},\mathcal{I}(v_{1})=\top\land\mathcal{I}(v_{2})=\top)\\
&=&\text{ite}((v_{1}^{\prime}=\text{Null}\land v_{2}^{\prime}=\text{Null})\land(v_{1}^{\prime}=\text{Null}\land v_{2}^{\prime}=\bot)\\
&&\qquad\land(v_{1}^{\prime}=\bot\land v_{2}^{\prime}=\text{Null}),\text{Null},v_{1}^{\prime}=\top\land v_{2}^{\prime}=\top)\\
&=&\llbracket\phi_{1}\land\phi_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}\end{array}

(9)

Inductive case:
\phi=\phi_{1}\lor\phi_{2}
.

\llbracket\phi_{1}\lor\phi_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}=\text{ite}((v_{1}=v_{2}=\text{Null})\lor(v_{1}=\text{Null}\land v_{2}=\bot)\lor(v_{1}=\bot\land v_{2}=\text{Null}),\text{Null},v_{1}=\top\lor v_{2}=\top)
where
v_{1}=\llbracket\phi_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}
and
v_{2}=\llbracket\phi_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}
by Figure
21
.

\llbracket\phi_{1}\lor\phi_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\text{ite}((v_{1}^{\prime}=v_{2}^{\prime}=\text{Null})\lor(v_{1}^{\prime}=\text{Null}\land v_{2}^{\prime}=\bot)\lor(v_{1}^{\prime}=\bot\land v_{2}^{\prime}=\text{Null}),\text{Null},v_{1}^{\prime}=\top\lor v_{2}^{\prime}=\top)
where
v_{1}^{\prime}=\llbracket\phi_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}
and
v_{2}^{\prime}=\llbracket\phi_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}
by Figure
17
.
By Lemma
D.1
, we have
\llbracket\phi_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\mathcal{I}(\llbracket\phi_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})
and
\llbracket\phi_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\mathcal{I}(\llbracket\phi_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})
.
Also since
\mathcal{I}(v_{1})=\mathcal{I}(\llbracket\phi_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})=\llbracket\phi_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=v_{1}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{I}(v_{2})=v_{2}^{\prime}
,

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathcal{I}(\llbracket\phi_{1}\lor\phi_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})&=&\mathcal{I}(\text{ite}((v_{1}=\text{Null}\lor v_{2}=\text{Null})\lor(v_{1}=\text{Null}\land v_{2}=\bot)\\
&&\qquad\lor(v_{1}=\bot\land v_{2}=\text{Null}),\text{Null},v_{1}=\top\lor v_{2}=\top))\\
&=&\text{ite}((\mathcal{I}(v_{1})=\text{Null}\lor\mathcal{I}(v_{2})=\text{Null})\lor(\mathcal{I}(v_{1})=\text{Null}\land\mathcal{I}(v_{2})=\bot)\\
&&\qquad\lor(\mathcal{I}(v_{1})=\bot\land\mathcal{I}(v_{2})=\text{Null}),\text{Null},\mathcal{I}(v_{1})=\top\lor\mathcal{I}(v_{2})=\top)\\
&=&\text{ite}((v_{1}^{\prime}=\text{Null}\lor v_{2}^{\prime}=\text{Null})\lor(v_{1}^{\prime}=\text{Null}\land v_{2}^{\prime}=\bot)\\
&&\qquad\lor(v_{1}^{\prime}=\bot\land v_{2}^{\prime}=\text{Null}),\text{Null},v_{1}^{\prime}=\top\lor v_{2}^{\prime}=\top)\\
&=&\llbracket\phi_{1}\lor\phi_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}\end{array}

(10)

Inductive case:
\phi=\neg\phi_{1}
.

\llbracket\neg\phi_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}=\textsf{ite}(\llbracket\phi_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}=\text{Null},\text{Null},\neg\llbracket\phi_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})
because
\llbracket\neg\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}=\text{let}~v=\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}\\

~\text{in}~\text{ite}(v=\text{Null},\text{Null},\neg v)
by Figure
21
.

\llbracket\neg\phi_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\textsf{ite}(\llbracket\phi_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\text{Null},\text{Null},\\

\neg\llbracket\phi_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})})
because
\llbracket\neg\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},xs}=\text{let}~v=\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},xs}~\text{in}~\text{ite}(v=\text{Null},\text{Null},\neg v)
by Figure
17
.
By Lemma
D.1
, we have
\llbracket\phi_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\mathcal{I}(\llbracket\phi_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})
.
Therefore,

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathcal{I}(\llbracket\neg\phi_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})&=&\textsf{ite}(\mathcal{I}(\llbracket\phi_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}=\text{Null})=\text{Null},\mathcal{I}(\neg\llbracket\phi_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}))\\
&=&\textsf{ite}(\mathcal{I}(\llbracket\phi_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})=\text{Null},\text{Null},\neg\mathcal{I}(\llbracket\phi_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}))\\
&=&\textsf{ite}(\llbracket\phi_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\text{Null},\text{Null},\neg\llbracket\phi_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})})\\
&=&\llbracket\neg\phi_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}\end{array}

∎

Lemma D.3.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a database over schema
\mathcal{S}
and
xs
be a tuple list which formulates an expression
E
.
Consider a symbolic database
\Gamma
over
\mathcal{S}
and a list of symbolic tuples
\mathcal{T}
such that
\llbracket E\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}
.
For any interpretation
\mathcal{I}
such that
\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)=\mathcal{D}\land\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})=xs
, evaluating
E
over the concrete database
\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)
and the concrete tuple list
\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})
yields the value of
\mathcal{I}(\llbracket E\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})
, i.e.,

\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)=\mathcal{D}\land\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})=xs\Rightarrow\llbracket E\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\mathcal{I}(\llbracket E\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})

Lemma D.4.

Suppose
\llbracket E\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},xs}=v
, then
\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)=\mathcal{D}\land\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})=xs\Rightarrow\llbracket E\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\mathcal{I}(\llbracket E\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})
is true iff
\llbracket E\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=v
and
\mathcal{I}(\llbracket E\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})=v
.

Proof.

By structural induction on
E
.

(1)

Base case:
E=a
.

\llbracket a\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}=\textsf{head}(\mathcal{T}).a
by Figure
22
.

\llbracket a\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=lookup(\textsf{head}(\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})),a)
by Figure
17
. Therefore,

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathcal{I}(\llbracket a\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})&=&\mathcal{I}(\textsf{head}(\mathcal{T}).a)=\mathcal{I}(lookup(\textsf{head}(\mathcal{T}),a))\\
&=&lookup(\textsf{head}(\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})),a)=lookup(\textsf{head}(xs),a)\\
&=&\llbracket a\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}\end{array}

(2)

Base case:
E=v
.

\llbracket v\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}=v
by Figure
22
.

\llbracket v\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=v
by Figure
17
. Therefore,
\mathcal{I}(\llbracket v\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})=\mathcal{I}(v)=v=\llbracket v\rrbracket_{\mathcal{D},xs}
.

(3)

Inductive case:
E=E_{1}\diamond E_{2}
.

\llbracket E_{1}\diamond E_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}=\textsf{ite}(v_{1}=\text{Null}\lor v_{2}=\text{Null},\text{Null},v_{1}\diamond v_{2})
where
v_{1}=\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}
and
v_{2}=\llbracket E_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}
by Figure
22
.

\llbracket E_{1}\diamond E_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\textsf{ite}(v_{1}^{\prime}=\text{Null}\lor v_{2}^{\prime}=\text{Null},\text{Null},v_{1}^{\prime}\diamond v_{2}^{\prime})
where
v_{1}^{\prime}=\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}
and
v_{2}^{\prime}=\llbracket E_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}
by Figure
17
.
By inductive hypothesis, we have
\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\mathcal{I}(\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})
and
\llbracket E_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\mathcal{I}(\llbracket E_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})
.
Also since
\mathcal{I}(v_{1})=\mathcal{I}(\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})=\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=v_{1}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{I}(v_{2})=v_{2}^{\prime}
,

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathcal{I}(\llbracket E_{1}\diamond E_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})&=&\mathcal{I}(\textsf{ite}(v_{1}=\text{Null}\lor v_{2}=\text{Null},\text{Null},v_{1}\diamond v_{2}))\\
&=&\textsf{ite}(\mathcal{I}(v_{1})=\text{Null}\lor\mathcal{I}(v_{2})=\text{Null},\text{Null},\mathcal{I}(v_{1})\diamond\mathcal{I}(v_{2}))\\
&=&\textsf{ite}(v_{1}^{\prime}=\text{Null}\lor v_{2}^{\prime}=\text{Null},\text{Null},v_{1}^{\prime}\diamond v_{2}^{\prime})\\
&=&\llbracket E_{1}\diamond E_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}\\
\end{array}

(4)

Inductive case:
E=\text{ITE}(\phi,E_{1},E_{2})
.

\llbracket\text{ITE}(\phi,E_{1},E_{2})\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}=\textsf{ite}(\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}=\top,\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}},\llbracket E_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})
by Figure
22
.

\llbracket\text{ITE}(\phi,E_{1},

E_{2})\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\textsf{ite}(\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\top,\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})},\llbracket E_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})})
by Figure
17
.
By inductive hypothesis, we have
\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\mathcal{I}(\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})
,
\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\mathcal{I}(\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})
and
\llbracket E_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\mathcal{I}(\llbracket E_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})
.
Therefore,

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathcal{I}(\llbracket\text{ITE}(\phi,E_{1},E_{2})\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})&=&\mathcal{I}(\textsf{ite}(\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}=\top,\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}},\llbracket E_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}))\\
&=&\textsf{ite}(\mathcal{I}(\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})=\top,\mathcal{I}(\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}),\mathcal{I}(\llbracket E_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}))\\
&=&\textsf{ite}(\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\top,\llbracket E_{1}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})},\llbracket E_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})})\\
&=&\llbracket\text{ITE}(\phi,E_{1},E_{2})\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}\\
\end{array}

(5)

Inductive case:
E=\text{Case}(\vec{\phi},\vec{E_{1}},E_{2})
.

\llbracket\text{Case}(\vec{\phi},\vec{E_{1}},E_{2})\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}=\llbracket\text{ITE}(\phi_{1},E_{1}^{1},\text{ITE}(\ldots,\text{ITE}(\phi_{n},E_{1}^{n},E_{2})))\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}
where
E_{1}^{i}\in\vec{E_{1}}
and
n=|\vec{\phi}|=|\vec{E}|
by Figure
22
.

\llbracket\text{Case}(\vec{\phi},\vec{E_{1}},E_{2})\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\textsf{foldr}(\lambda y.\lambda(\phi_{i},E_{1}^{i}).\llbracket\text{ite}(\phi_{i},E_{1}^{i},y)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})},

E_{2},reverse(zip(\vec{\phi},\vec{E_{1}})))
by Figure
17
.
By inductive hypothesis, we have
\llbracket\phi_{i}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\mathcal{I}(\llbracket\phi_{i}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})
,
\llbracket E_{1}^{i}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\mathcal{I}(\llbracket E_{1}^{i}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})
and
\llbracket E_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\mathcal{I}(\llbracket E_{2}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})
.

∎

Lemma D.5.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a database over schema
\mathcal{S}
and
xs
be a tuple list which formulates an attribute
A
.
Consider a symbolic database
\Gamma
over
\mathcal{S}
and a list of symbolic tuples
\mathcal{T}
such that
\llbracket A\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}
.
For any interpretation
\mathcal{I}
such that
\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)=\mathcal{D}\land\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})=xs
, evaluating
A
over the concrete database
\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)
and the concrete tuple list
\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})
yields the value of
\mathcal{I}(\llbracket A\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})
, i.e.,

\mathcal{I}(\Gamma)=\mathcal{D}\land\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})=xs\Rightarrow\llbracket A\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\mathcal{I}(\llbracket A\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})

Proof.

By structural induction on
A
.

(1)

Inductive case:
A=\text{Cast}(\phi)
.

By the definition of the
Cast
function, we know
\text{Cast}(\phi)=\text{Null}
iff
\phi=\text{Null}
,
\text{Cast}(\phi)=1
iff
\phi
is true; otherwise it returns 0.
Furthermore, by Lemma
D.3
, we know
\mathcal{I}(\llbracket A\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})=\mathcal{I}(\llbracket\text{Cast}(\phi)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})=\llbracket\text{Cast}(\phi)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\llbracket v\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\llbracket E\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\llbracket A\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}
where
v\in\{0,1\}
if
\phi\in\{\top,\bot\}
; and by Lemma
D.2

\mathcal{I}(\llbracket A\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})=\mathcal{I}(\llbracket\text{Cast}(\text{Null})\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})=\llbracket\text{Cast}(\text{Null})\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\llbracket\text{Null}\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\llbracket\phi\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\llbracket A\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}
if
\phi=\text{Null}
.

(2)

Inductive case:
A=E
.

By Lemma
D.3
, we know
\mathcal{I}(\llbracket A\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})=\mathcal{I}(\llbracket E\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})=\llbracket E\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\llbracket A\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}
.

(3)

Inductive case:
A=\text{Count}(E)
.

\llbracket\text{Count}(E)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}}=\textsf{ite}(\text{AllNull}(E,\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}),\text{Null},\Sigma_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\text{ite}(\text{Del}(t)\lor\llbracket E\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t]}=\text{Null},0,1))
by Figure
22
.

\llbracket\text{Count}(E)\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\textsf{ite}(\text{AllNull}(E,\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})),\text{Null},\textsf{foldl}(+,0,\textsf{map}(xs,\lambda y.

\textsf{ite}(\llbracket E\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),[y]}=\text{Null},0,1))))
by Figure
17
.
By Lemma
D.3
, we have
\llbracket E\rrbracket_{\mathcal{I}(\Gamma),\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}=\\

\mathcal{I}(\llbracket E\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}})
.
By the definition of
\mathcal{I}
,
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t)=\top
for the symbolic tuple
t\in\mathcal{T}\land t\not\in xs
; similarly
\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t)=\bot
for the symbolic tuple
t\in\mathcal{T}\land t\in xs
.

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathcal{I}(\text{AllNull}(E,\mathcal{S},\Gamma,\mathcal{T}))&=&\mathcal{I}(\land_{t\in\mathcal{T}}(\neg\text{Del}(t)\to\llbracket E\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t]}=\text{Null}))\\
&=&\land_{t\in\mathcal{I}(\mathcal{T})}(\neg\mathcal{I}(\text{Del})(t)\to\mathcal{I}(\llbracket E\rrbracket_{\mathcal{S},\Gamma,[t]})=\text{Null})\\
